Excusing Ignorance and Interpretive Error
The Ruling on the Ignorant and on One Who Errs by Interpretation
Translated from the Arabic: العذر بالجهل والتأويل


[Page 1]

(Excusing Ignorance and Interpretive Error)
(Introduction) One of the reasons for this study is to bring about reconciliation among groups of Muslims: between the group that does not excuse someone who, despite having testified to the two testimonies of faith and embraced faith in general terms, interprets the matter in a way that has some basis in the Sharia or the Arabic language, and therefore declares such people disbelievers and declares those who excuse them disbelievers; and the other group, which excuses those who interpret matters in this way among Ahl al-Sunna, but nevertheless does not excuse the first group for declaring such people disbelievers, accusing them of being Kharijites and applying to them the hadiths of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) concerning the Kharijites. Is someone from among the people of the qibla who makes every effort in interpretation truly a disbeliever? And is someone who declares him a disbeliever and does not excuse him a Kharijite? This is what the researcher seeks to answer. As for any error in reasoning in this study, I retract it, in my lifetime and after my death. I seek only to set things right as far as I am able, and my success is only through Allah.
Among the great deeds that Islam encourages and urges people to undertake is reconciliation between people. Islam is a religion of love, unity, and harmony among Muslims. Allah, Exalted is He, says: ﴾There is no good in much of their private conversation, except for those who enjoin charity, kindness, or reconciliation between people. Whoever does this, seeking Allah's pleasure, We shall grant him a great reward.﴿ [al-Nisa: 114].
Imam al-Tabari, may Allah have mercy on him, said regarding "or reconciliation between people": "It is reconciliation between those at odds or in dispute, in a manner that Allah has permitted, so that they may return to what brings affection and unity of purpose, in accordance with what Allah has permitted and commanded." (1)


[Page 2]

The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) also informed us that whoever reconciles people will be granted a tremendous reward. Imam Ahmad narrated in his Musnad, from the hadith of Abu al-Darda (may Allah be pleased with him), that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Shall I tell you what is better in rank than prayer, fasting, and charity?" They said, "Yes." He said, "Reconciling people, for discord among people is the shaver." (2) In another narration: "I do not mean that it shaves hair; rather, it shaves away religion." (3)

Through reconciliation, the brotherhood of Islam is realized. Allah, Exalted is He, says: ﴾The believers are but brothers, so make peace between your two brothers, and fear Allah so that you may be shown mercy.﴿ [al-Hujurat: 10].
I ask Allah to envelop us all in His mercy. There is no power nor strength except through Allah.

Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful, Master of the Day of Judgment. O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed the family of Abraham, and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed the family of Abraham. You are indeed Praiseworthy, Glorious. O Allah, guide us by Your permission to the truth concerning which people have differed; indeed, You guide whom You will to a straight path.
The scholars differed concerning the ruling on the ignorant person. We shall begin by mentioning their views. There is no power nor strength except through Allah.


[Page 3]

First, some held that ignorance is never an excuse in matters of belief. They based this on the fact that Allah created people upon the religion of Islam, namely, monotheism. By this, they meant singling out Allah in worship, which is obedience, as well as the forms this obedience takes. For example, in their view, the ugliness of prostrating to anyone other than Allah is established by reason and by innate disposition. Likewise, some of Allah's attributes, such as knowledge, power, and life, are, in their view, known by innate disposition. Some included hearing and sight among what is known by innate disposition, while others, such as al-Tabari, did not. Although this group, which has a hadith-based strand, includes attributes of Allah such as hearing and sight among what is known by innate disposition, they did not declare al-Tabari an unbeliever for this, since he excused ignorance of the attributes of hearing and sight. In their view, one who is ignorant of an attribute known by innate disposition is an unbeliever; yet they did not declare him an unbeliever. Rather, they often cited his statements in support of the view that ignorance is no excuse. This is one of the contradictions that emerged among them, for al-Tabari says: "Al-Tabari said in his book al-Tabsir...
((Abu Ja'far said:
As for that without knowledge of which, in our view, no one's profession of faith is valid, and without which the ruling of disbelief is not removed from him, it is what we have already mentioned. (He had mentioned that He is the Creator and Disposer of affairs, that there is nothing like Him, and that He is powerful, speaking, and knowing.)
That is because no one who has reached the age of legal responsibility is excused for being ignorant of the attributes we mentioned earlier, whether a messenger from Allah, Exalted is He, has come to him or not, and whether he has seen any of creation besides himself or has seen no one but himself.
Allah, exalted is He, has names and attributes that His Book has stated and with which His Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) informed his community. No one among Allah's creation for whom the proof has been established—that the Quran was revealed with them and that the Messenger of Allah's statement concerning them has been authentically established through the reports transmitted from him—may contradict them. If he does so after the proof has been established against him through the reports, in the manner I have explained concerning that whose true nature there is no way to know


[Page 4]

The one ignorant of it is excused on account of his ignorance, for knowledge of this cannot be attained through reason, reflection, or thought.
This includes what Allah, Exalted is He, has informed us: that He is All-Hearing, All-Seeing, and that He has two hands, as He says: {Rather, both His hands are outstretched}; and that He has a right hand, as He says: {And the heavens will be folded in His right hand}; and that He has a face, as He says: {Everything will perish except His face}, and: {And there will remain the face of your Lord, Possessor of majesty and honor}; and that He has a foot, as the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Until the Lord places His foot in it"—meaning Hell.
And that He laughs at His believing servant, as the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said of the man who was killed in the path of Allah: "He met Allah, Mighty and Majestic, while He was laughing at him."
And that He descends every night to the lowest heaven, according to the report of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him).
And that He is not one-eyed, as the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said when mentioning the Dajjal: "He is one-eyed, but your Lord is not one-eyed." And that the believers will see their Lord on the Day of Resurrection with their eyes, just as they see the sun when no cloud veils it, and just as they see the full moon, according to the words of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him). And that He has fingers, as the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "There is no heart except that it is between two of the fingers of the Most Merciful."
These meanings that have been described, and others like them, are among the things with which Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has described Himself, or with which His Messenger (peace and blessings be upon him) has described Him. Their true nature cannot be grasped through thought or reflection. We do not declare anyone an unbeliever for being ignorant of them unless they have been conveyed to him.


[Page 5]

"If the report transmitted concerning that is one by which proof is established, taking the place of seeing and hearing, then the listener is obliged to affirm its truth and testify that the report conveyed it, just as he would testify to the truth of what he saw and heard."
Therefore, according to them, one who is ignorant of an attribute is an unbeliever if that attribute is known by innate disposition (fitra). They did not set a limit on what is known by innate disposition as opposed to what is not, and this led to their differing over whether one who is ignorant of some attributes is to be declared an unbeliever. For these people, the innate disposition upon which people were created encompasses, more or less, the religion of Islam and its branches. They hold that Allah fashioned the intellect to deem monotheism good and polytheism bad. I say "its forms" because some have said that only monotheism itself is established by innate disposition, meaning that Allah alone has the right to be worshipped—that He alone is to be obeyed. But, in their view, the forms of monotheism and the forms of polytheism are not established by reason, but by transmitted evidence. For example, prostrating to someone other than Allah is not, in their view, polytheism unless there is a textual proof, since prostration to Adam and Joseph is established, yet it was neither polytheism nor unbelief. This group held that proof is established against people by innate disposition, while some of them restricted what is known by innate disposition to the matter of monotheism and polytheism. As for other practical matters, and likewise some less apparent matters of unbelief, these are not established by innate disposition in their view, so one who is ignorant of them is excused. Among the proofs they cite is the hadith that every newborn is born upon the innate disposition, and in one narration, "upon this religion." Thus, they say, proof was established against people in this way. They also said that Allah, Exalted is He, judged the Prophet's father and mother, Ibn Jud'an, and Amr ibn Luhayy to be unbelievers, even though they lived during an interval between messengers, in light of His saying, "so that you may warn a people whose forefathers were not warned, and so they are heedless," and His saying, "We sent no warner to them before you." This indicates that they were held accountable on the basis of innate disposition or reason. There is no difference between an original polytheist and someone who became Muslim and then committed polytheism: neither is excused. Allah, Exalted is He, also said, "Are your unbelievers better than those, or have you been granted immunity in the scriptures?" There is likewise no difference in the ruling between one unbeliever and another. They also said that reason recognizes the repugnance of things before the advent of the revealed law. Thus, anyone who commits a sin, whether in the matter of


[Page 6]

Beliefs or actions. Some restricted it to beliefs, in which case one is punished for it and has no excuse. Others restricted this to the matter of divine oneness and what pertains to it. As for actions, these are not deemed obligatory by reason, according to them, but only through revealed texts; one who commits them is excused until he is able to obtain the legal proof, or until the legal proof actually reaches him.


[Page 7]

Among their proofs was that Allah called certain sins sins and wrongdoing before sending the messengers. The Exalted said: (And if any of the polytheists seeks your protection, then grant him protection so that he may hear the words of Allah.) Thus He called him a polytheist before he had heard the words of Allah. Likewise, Allah's saying: (And when your Lord took from the children of Adam, from their loins, their descendants, and made them testify against themselves: "Am I not your Lord?" They said, "Yes indeed; we testify"—lest you should say on the Day of Resurrection, "We were unaware of this.") (172) The fact that He, Exalted is He, said to them what means, "so that you do not say on the Day of Resurrection, 'We were unaware of this,'" is proof that He did this so it would be evidence against them, which they would recall on the Day of Resurrection. This included all the descendants of Adam: Allah brought them forth from his loins and made them testify against themselves. Their testimony that He is their Lord means that they knew He is the Creator, Provider, Giver of life and death, and Disposer of affairs; and that He is also Living; and that there would also be a Day of Resurrection, as indicated by His saying before them, "the Day of Resurrection." These divine attributes, and others understood from the verse, were therefore known to them at the time of this testimony. Hence, no one has an excuse for being ignorant of them, and whoever is ignorant of them is an unbeliever and is not excused on account of ignorance, because there is no ignorance in the first place.
This group included Allah's attributes in this category, so they did not excuse anyone ignorant of an attribute, nor anyone who interpreted an attribute figuratively. In their view, such an interpreter is merely doubly ignorant, so his excuse is of no avail. Thus they declared all the groups that interpret the attributes figuratively, including the Ash'aris and others, to be unbelievers, because they say that Allah's attributes are apprehended by innate disposition. They likewise declared to be unbelievers all those who excuse people who interpret the attributes figuratively. In their view, whoever excuses the Ash'aris is an unbeliever, because he has called an unbeliever a Muslim; and this is unbelief, since whoever does not declare an unbeliever to be an unbeliever is himself an unbeliever.
They argued that reason can discern what is reprehensible even without a messenger being sent, citing several proofs, including: ({And when your Lord called Moses: "Go to the wrongdoing people, the people of Pharaoh. Will they not be mindful of Allah?"}) and His saying: ({Indeed, Pharaoh exalted himself in the land and divided its people into factions, oppressing a group among them, slaughtering their sons and sparing their women. Indeed, he was one of the corrupters.}) He called them wrongdoers, transgressors, and corrupters before sending the messenger. And He, Exalted is He, said: (Go to Pharaoh; indeed, he has transgressed.) His saying that he and his people were wrongdoers, transgressors, and corrupters is a condemnation of their actions, and condemnation applies only to evil, reprehensible actions. This shows that actions can be reprehensible and blameworthy before a messenger comes to them, and thus proves that they are held accountable by innate disposition or by reason.


[Page 8]

Likewise, He related that Hud said to his people: (Worship Allah; you have no god other than Him. You are only fabricators.) He thus called them fabricators before issuing a ruling that they would contravene, because they had set up another god alongside Allah. Likewise, His saying, Exalted is He: (Say, "Allah does not command indecency. Do you say about Allah what you do not know?" (28)) shows that indecency is known by reason. Otherwise, if indecency meant only what Allah has forbidden—since indecency could be known only through textual revelation, not reason—the verse would mean, "Allah does not command what He has forbidden," and this is false. This shows that abominations, the greatest of which is associating partners with Allah, are known by innate disposition and reason before a prohibition comes in revelation, so no one who is ignorant of them has an excuse. The Noble Quran frequently argues from the disbelievers' acknowledgment of His lordship, Exalted and Glorified is He, to the obligation of singling Him out in worship. Thus, when addressing them about the oneness of lordship, He asks them a question that elicits their affirmation. Once they acknowledge His lordship, He uses it as proof against them that He alone deserves to be worshipped, and rebukes them, condemning their association of others with Him despite their acknowledgment that He alone is the Lord. For whoever acknowledges that Allah, Exalted is He, alone is the Lord must also acknowledge that He alone deserves to be worshipped. Examples of this include His saying, Exalted is He: (Say, "Who provides for you from the heaven and the earth, or who controls hearing and sight?") up to His saying: (They will say, "Allah.") When they acknowledged His lordship, He rebuked them, condemning their association of others with Him, saying: (Then say, "Will you not be mindful of Allah?") Another example is His saying, Exalted is He: (Say, "To whom belong the earth and all who are in it, if you know?" They will say, "To Allah.") When they acknowledged this, He rebuked them, condemning their association of others with Him, saying: (Say, "Will you not then reflect?") Then He said: (Say, "Who is the Lord of the seven heavens and the Lord of the Mighty Throne?" They will say, "Allah.") When they acknowledged this, He rebuked them, condemning their association of others with Him, saying: (Say, "Will you not be mindful of Allah?") Then He said: (Say, "In whose hand is the dominion of all things, and who protects, while none can protect against Him, if you know?" They will say, "Allah.") When they acknowledged this, He rebuked them, condemning their association of others with Him, saying: (How, then, are you deluded?) Another example is His saying, Exalted is He: (Say, "Who is the Lord of the heavens and the earth?" Say, "Allah.") When their acknowledgment was established, He rebuked them, condemning their association of others with Him, saying: (Say, "Have you then taken protectors besides Him, who have no power to benefit or harm themselves?")
And His saying, Exalted is He: (If you ask them, "Who created the heavens and the earth and subjected the sun and the moon...")


[Page 9]

"They will surely say, 'Allah.'" Once their acknowledgment was established, He rebuked them, denouncing their associating partners with Him, saying: "How, then, are they turned away?" And His saying, Exalted is He: "If you ask them, 'Who sent down water from the sky and thereby brought the earth back to life after its death?' they will surely say, 'Allah.'" Once their acknowledgment was established, He rebuked them, denouncing their associating partners with Him, saying: "Say, 'Praise be to Allah.' But most of them do not reason." And His saying: "If you ask them, 'Who created the heavens and the earth?' they will surely say, 'Allah.'" Once their acknowledgment was established, He rebuked them, denouncing them, saying: "Say, 'Praise be to Allah.' But most of them do not know." And His saying, Exalted is He: "Is Allah better, or what they associate with Him? Or who created the heavens and the earth and sent down water for you from the sky, with which We caused gardens of delight to grow? You could not have caused their trees to grow." There is no doubt that the only answer they could possibly give is that the One capable of creating the heavens and the earth, and all that He mentions alongside them, is better than an inanimate object incapable of doing anything. Once their acknowledgment was thus established, He rebuked them, denouncing their associating partners with Him, saying: "Is there a god alongside Allah? Rather, they are a people who deviate." There are very many verses of this kind. For this reason, we have stated elsewhere that all questions concerning the oneness of lordship (tawhid al-rububiyya) are interrogative questions intended to elicit acknowledgment; once they acknowledge it, they are then rebuked and censured on the basis of that acknowledgment, because one who acknowledges lordship must necessarily acknowledge divinity (uluhiyya), as in His saying, Exalted is He: "Is there any doubt about Allah?" And His saying: "Say, 'Shall I seek a lord other than Allah?'" End quote. They likewise said that Allah rebuked the polytheists for failing to acknowledge divinity despite acknowledging lordship.
But this group differed among themselves. One faction declared those who committed polytheism to be unbelievers and said that they are punished in this world by being fought, and in the Hereafter they will remain forever in the Fire of Hell, since proof was established against them by reason and innate disposition, even if no messenger came to them or a messenger's message did not reach them. This is based on the aforementioned punishment of Amr ibn Luhayy and the Prophet's father (peace and blessings be upon him), despite His saying, Exalted is He: "No warner had come to them before you." The other faction said: We apply the terms polytheism and unbelief to them in this world, just as Allah applied them to these ignorant people to whom no messenger came; but punishment in this world and the Hereafter occurs only after messengers have been sent, and after the test on the Day of Resurrection, as in the hadith of al-Aswad ibn Sari.


[Page 10]

Imam Ahmad (may Allah have mercy on him) said (vol. 4, p. 24): Ali narrated to us; Muadh ibn Hisham narrated to us; he said: My father narrated to me, from Qatada, from al-Ahnaf ibn Qays, from al-Aswad ibn Sari: The Prophet of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said: "There are four on the Day of Resurrection: a deaf man who hears nothing, an imbecile, an elderly man, and one who died during an interval [between prophets]. The deaf man will say, 'My Lord, Islam came, but I could hear nothing.' The imbecile will say, 'My Lord, Islam came, while the children were pelting me with camel dung.' The elderly man will say, 'My Lord, Islam came, but I could understand nothing.' And the one who died during the interval will say, 'My Lord, no messenger of Yours came to me.' He will take their pledges that they will obey Him, then send word to them: 'Enter the Fire.' He said: By Him in whose hand is Muhammad's soul, if they entered it, it would be coolness and safety for them."
Ali narrated to us; Muadh ibn Hisham narrated to us; he said: My father narrated to me, from Qatada, from al-Hasan, from Abu Rafi, from Abu Hurayra, something similar, except that at the end he said: "Whoever enters it, it will be coolness and safety for him; and whoever does not enter it will be dragged to it."
This is an authentic hadith; its narrators are among the narrators of the Sahih collections." They said: "That is, the people before the sending of a messenger and the establishment of proof are excused with regard to some rulings, but not excused with regard to others.
They are excused in that they are not punished in this world or the Hereafter until the prophetic proof is established against them—and this is from Allah's mercy and grace.
They are not excused for committing polytheism and the rulings that follow from it, such as not burying them in Muslim cemeteries, not praying over them, and not tending their graves.


[Page 11]

and seeking forgiveness for them; their slaughtered animals are not to be eaten, their women are not to be married, and they will not enter Paradise until they are tested. Likewise, fighting and jihad against the polytheists may take place only after the proof has been established, though before that they are polytheists." Thus, as you can see, they distinguished between merely applying the name and the rulings concerning punishment in the Hereafter and worldly punishment by killing. Another group said that the name entails punishment in the Hereafter, but not worldly punishment by killing and captivity. They said that as long as the name applies, its rulings in the Hereafter apply: they will be punished in the Hereafter. As for the words of the Exalted, "We would never punish until We had sent a messenger," what is meant is their punishment in this world. Al-Shawkani transmitted this view from the majority. As for the Hereafter, they will be punished in the Fire, because the Prophet's father was punished, despite the words of the Exalted, "No warner had come to them before you."
Another group distinguished between someone who was originally a polytheist and someone who entered Islam and then committed polytheism. They said: an original polytheist is called a polytheist and is punished in this world by being treated as one; as for the Hereafter, his case depends on whether a messenger came to him or not. If no messenger came to him, he will be tested on the Day of Resurrection—as stated in the hadith—with a fire into which he will be commanded to enter. Whoever enters it will find it cool and safe, and whoever does not enter it will be dragged into it." This is based on the hadith, "No one from this community, whether Jew or Christian, hears of me and then fails to believe in me without being among the people of the Fire." As for someone who has testified to the two testimonies of faith, believed in general terms in everything the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) brought, and has not neglected seeking knowledge, then exercised his judgment and erred in committing one form of polytheism or disbelief, he is excused in that, because the issues


[Page 12]

Shirk, disbelief, and their forms, according to this group, are not known through reason; they are known only through the Sharia. Reason has no role here at all. Thus, reason provides no proof here for knowing what constitutes disbelief and what constitutes shirk. In their view, these people are excused if they have not been negligent in seeking knowledge and have some kind of interpretation concerning an aspect of shirk or the divine attributes. This is due to a special quality of this community: the sin of error has been lifted from it, as in the verse, {There is no blame upon you for what you do by mistake}, and the hadith, "If a judge exercises independent judgment and is correct, he receives two rewards; if he exercises independent judgment and errs, he receives one reward." In the language, a judge is anyone who issues a ruling on a matter and then believes it or acts upon it. Thus, it includes anyone who acts or holds a belief, and so encompasses error in action or belief.
Among their proofs were the hadith of Dhat Anwat, the man who scattered his own ashes, and also the hadith of Aisha: "Whatever people conceal, Allah knows it." He said, "Yes." When they were asked, "Why did you distinguish between an original polytheist and a Muslim from whom shirk has occurred, when they are the same?" they replied, "Allah is the one who distinguished between them in what has already been mentioned, and reason has no role here." This is like what some have said: that committing shirk under coercion was not excused, based on their saying, {If they discover you, they will stone you or force you back into their religion}. They did not present a third option, which indicates that coercion was not considered in the case of shirk. But in our Sharia, committing shirk by word or deed under coercion incurs no sin and is excused. Likewise, error in this matter is also excused for this community in particular. This is also suggested by the hadith: "Ibn Abbas (may Allah be pleased with them both) reported that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) said: 'Allah has pardoned my community for error…'"


[Page 13]

"...and forgetfulness and what they were compelled to do." They said that earlier nations were held accountable for their mistakes and called to account for all their actions, with neither ignorance nor forgetfulness serving as an excuse for them or a reason to pardon them. But these burdens have been lifted from this nation in response to their supplication and as a mercy from Allah to them, as Allah, Exalted is He, made clear in His words: {Our Lord, do not take us to task if we forget or make a mistake. Our Lord, do not place upon us a burden as You placed upon those before us. Our Lord, do not burden us with what we have no strength to bear} (al-Baqara: 286), and His words: {There is no blame upon you for what you do by mistake, but only for what your hearts intend. And Allah is All-Forgiving, Most Merciful} (al-Ahzab: 5). It is thus clear that there is no problem in distinguishing between an original disbeliever and a Muslim who has committed a form of shirk. However, the first group, who do not excuse shirk or any of its forms, responded to this group on this particular point by saying that as soon as a Muslim engages in a form of shirk, he is a polytheist at the moment he engages in it, and is called a polytheist for the reasons already mentioned. Consequently, none of the preceding verses or hadith applies to him. Rather, the preceding verses and hadith apply to someone who has committed something short of shirk, or something obscure among matters of disbelief; yet they did not set a limit for this obscure disbelief. Therefore, those who excuse people on grounds of ignorance have held that someone who commits a form of shirk or disbelief on the basis of an interpretation is not thereby deemed a disbeliever, because the forms of shirk are not established by reason: sometimes prostrating to someone other than Allah is shirk, and sometimes it is merely a greeting. In this case, the ruling of Islam remains applicable to him, and the preceding verses and hadith apply to him. However, this group that excuses the latter ignorant person...


[Page 14]

He laid down conditions for this excuse. The first is that the ignorant person must not have been negligent in seeking knowledge or turned away from it, for in that case he is not called ignorant or mistaken. Rather, he knows the path of truth but does not follow it, and deliberately turns away from the evidence. The verse therefore applies to him: {And those who disbelieve turn away from that of which they were warned.} He is subject to the punishment of a polytheist if he commits an act of polytheism, as judged by the rulings of this world. The punishments reported from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) for those who died during the interval between prophets, such as the punishment of his father, peace be upon him, and the punishment of Amr ibn Luhayy, can be understood in two ways. The first is that remnants of the religion of Abraham had reached them, but they turned away from it. There was someone living among them, Zayd ibn Amr ibn Nufayl, who died following the religion of Abraham; he sought it and found it. So they have no excuse. The second is that the religion of Abraham had not reached them, but they turned away from seeking it. Their ignorance was thus due to their turning away, and one who turns away is not excused—especially since Zayd ibn Amr ibn Nufayl had sought the truth and found it. Likewise, whoever died during the interval without knowledge and then embraced polytheism was, in reality, fabricating lies about Allah, for he merely followed his forefathers in their beliefs based on conjecture, and conjecture is the most false of speech. Their conjecture was a lie about Allah for which they are held accountable. Since they found no one to teach them, they should have remained firm in what they were certain of: that Allah, Glorified and Exalted is He, alone is divine. They should have said, "O Allah, if we knew of a religion better than this, we would follow it," as Zayd ibn Amr ibn Nufayl said something similar. Either they fabricated about Allah what they did not know, following conjecture without proof—in which case they deliberately committed polytheism and spoke lies about Allah—or, as Allah Most High said: {And whoever calls upon besides ...}


[Page 15]

…another god alongside Allah, for which he has no proof. His reckoning is with his Lord. Indeed, the disbelievers do not prosper." Thus, He declared anyone who invokes anyone besides Allah without proof to be a disbeliever. In this respect, they are not excused. Those mentioned in the hadith as being punished, such as the Prophet's father (peace and blessings be upon him) and Amr ibn Luhayy, belong to this group; these conditions were met in their case, so punishment was due to them.
However, this group that excuses them split into two groups. One group said that it is sufficient for the proof to be established that a person has the ability to learn. If a person is able to acquire knowledge but falls short in doing so and commits an act of shirk, then he is a disbelieving polytheist, because he is no longer considered ignorant or mistaken, but rather one who turns away from the truth and commits disbelief through turning away. As you can see, this criterion is not well-defined, since the ability to acquire knowledge is relative. Thus, they said that a recent convert to Islam and someone raised in a remote desert are not deemed disbelievers, because they are unable to acquire knowledge. Since this criterion is not well-defined, rulings on declaring some people who engage in major shirk to be disbelievers have differed in certain countries, depending on whether or not they are considered to have had the ability to acquire knowledge. As for the other group that excuses them, they said that the ability to seek knowledge is not sufficient for the proof to be established; rather, the proof must reach the person legally accountable before he can be declared a disbeliever. If one of them is able to acquire knowledge but turns away from it, then falls into shirk while being a Muslim, he is not judged to be a polytheist, because the proof did not reach him. He is only sinful for falling short in learning what is obligatory for him. They said, "Failing to seek knowledge of the details despite being able to do so does not, by itself, entail disbelief, unless its cause is aversion to what one has previously learned, contempt for it, or arrogance toward it."


[Page 16]

At that point, a violation of submission is joined to it. As for failure to learn in itself, it does not entail denial, since it may be due to inattention, never having turned one's thoughts to the question, or culpable negligence that does not indicate hatred or arrogance—as is the case with many Muslims regarding many details. If it were said that the criterion is the ability to learn, then the masses would have to be declared unbelievers merely because they are ignorant despite being able to acquire knowledge. Indeed, the Companions' knowledge during the Prophet's lifetime did not encompass every detail; otherwise, they too would have to be declared unbelievers, even though Allah has declared them upright and is pleased with them. Likewise, one who enters Islam—with a general understanding of the two testimonies of faith and a readiness to submit to the details—would be an unbeliever until he learned those details. He would remain an unbeliever for a period until he learned them, because he could learn them immediately after uttering the two testimonies, without any interval. And if he learned them over a period of time, he would be an unbeliever because he could have learned them in a shorter period but did not! End quote. However, their words apply to someone who has embraced faith in general and uttered the two testimonies, then remained ignorant of the details and refrained from speaking about them until he learned them, without speaking about what he did not know. For example, Zayd ibn Amr ibn Nufayl believed in Allah in general and that He alone deserves worship, and stopped there. He did not speak about Allah without knowledge, nor did he falsely attribute anything to Allah by entering into polytheistic rites without knowledge, for by doing so he would falsely attribute to Allah an act of worship that He had not prescribed. In such a case, he would outwardly appear to be excused. The discussion, rather, concerns someone who turns away from learning, then follows conjecture in attributing to Allah what he does not attribute to Himself, through various forms of polytheistic worship or other kinds of unbelief, or denies something of what Allah has revealed without knowing it, following conjecture or desire. Whether he is excused in this situation is questionable, because here he has transgressed, for he…


[Page 17]

Allah has stated that whoever cannot produce sound evidence for what he says or does is following his own desires. He, Exalted is He, says: ((Say, "Then bring a Book from Allah that is a better guide than these two, and I will follow it, if you are truthful." 49 But if they do not respond to you, then know that they are only following their own desires. And who is more astray than one who follows his own desires without guidance from Allah? Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people." 50)) Thus, He, Exalted is He, called the one who brings no evidence for what he says a follower of his own desires, declared that none is more astray than him, and described him as one of the wrongdoers. All of this entails declaring him an unbeliever.
And He says: ((Indeed, those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah without any authority that has come to them have nothing in their hearts but pride, which they will never attain. So seek refuge in Allah; indeed, He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing. 56)) Thus, He, Exalted is He, described the one who disputes without knowledge as having pride: by Allah's description, he is arrogant, a wrongdoer, and astray.
And He, Exalted is He, says: ((They say, "Allah has taken a son." Glory be to Him! He is the Self-Sufficient; to Him belongs all that is in the heavens and all that is on the earth. You have no authority for this. Do you say about Allah what you do not know? 68 Say, "Indeed, those who fabricate lies about Allah will not succeed." 69 A brief enjoyment in this world, then to Us will be their return; then We will make them taste the severe punishment for their disbelief. 70)) Thus, He, Exalted is He, declared that those who fabricate lies about Allah will not succeed, and indicated that by doing so they disbelieve.
And He, Exalted is He, says: ((And among the people is one who disputes about Allah without knowledge, guidance, or an illuminating Book, (8) turning his neck aside to lead others away from the way of Allah. For him there is disgrace in this world, and on the Day of Resurrection We will make him taste the punishment of the Burning Fire. (9))) Thus, He, Exalted is He, made clear that the one who disputes without knowledge is punished and burdened with sin.
And he (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "And a man who did not know the truth and judged among people out of ignorance is in the Fire." Thus, he declared that the ignorant person who speaks without knowledge is in the Fire.
And He said: ((These are our people; they have taken gods besides Him. Why do they not bring clear authority for them? Who is more unjust than one who fabricates a lie about Allah? (15))) He called the one who brings no authority or proof a fabricator of lies about Allah, and declared that none is more unjust than him; this indicates his unbelief.
And He said: ((Those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah without any authority that has come to them: it is greatly hateful to Allah and to those who believe. Thus Allah seals the heart of every arrogant tyrant.)) Thus, He called the one who disputes without authority hateful.
And he (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Whoever relates a hadith from me, believing it to be a lie, is one of the liars." Thus, he called the person who thought the hadith was false and transmitted it, attributing it to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him), a liar—even if he was not certain—and called him a liar.
And He, Exalted is He, says: ((Those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah without any authority that has come to them: it is greatly hateful to Allah and to those who believe. Thus Allah seals the heart of every arrogant tyrant.)) Thus, He called the one who disputes out of ignorance hateful.
From this, you can see that there are two men: one who has secured his general faith, then remains silent about what he does not know, saying, "If Allah and His Messenger said it, then I believe it." He has not invented anything about Allah or any religious law and attributed it to Allah, whether he invented it himself or followed someone else in it, because one who follows another is himself speaking without


[Page 18]

This is speaking about Allah without knowledge, and it is forbidden; its perpetrator has no excuse. The blind following of those who cited their forefathers did not avail them at all.
Allah, Exalted is He, says: {Thus We did not send before you any warner to a town without its affluent people saying, "We found our fathers following a way, and we are following in their footsteps."} (23)
Thus, there is a difference between someone who has embraced faith in general terms and then does not invent anything—whether deliberately or by blind following—that contradicts that faith, thereby speaking about Allah without knowledge; and someone who remains silent about that and says, "I believe in everything the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) brought, and I do not speak about Allah's religion without knowledge." These are two different things.
Ibn Hazm said: ("Al-Ihkam fi Usul al-Ahkam by Ibn Hazm" (6/12):
"Among the things that utterly invalidate their position are the words of Allah, Exalted is He: {And do not say, as to what your tongues falsely describe, 'This is lawful and this is forbidden,' in order to fabricate lies against Allah. Indeed, those who fabricate lies against Allah will not prosper.} And His words, Exalted is He: {Say, 'Have you considered the provision Allah has sent down for you, of which you have made some forbidden and some lawful?' Say, 'Has Allah given you permission, or are you fabricating lies against Allah?'} These two verses establish that anyone who declares lawful or forbidden something without permission from Allah, Exalted is He, to forbid or permit it, has fabricated a lie against Allah. We are certain that Allah, Exalted is He, has made lawful for us everything He created on earth, except what He explicitly specified as forbidden, in accordance with His words, Exalted is He: {And why should you not eat of that over which Allah's name has been mentioned, when He has clearly explained to you what He has forbidden you, except when you are compelled to do so? Indeed, many lead others astray through their desires, without knowledge. Your Lord knows best those who transgress.} These two clear texts thus invalidate the forbidding of anything by anyone on the pretext of precaution or fear."
And he said: ("Al-Ihkam fi Usul al-Ahkam by Ibn Hazm" (1/22):
"He praised the People of the Cave: {We relate to you their story in truth. They were youths who believed in their Lord, and We increased them in guidance. And We strengthened their hearts when they stood up and said, 'Our Lord is the Lord of the heavens and the earth. We will never call upon any god besides Him, for then we would have uttered an outrageous falsehood. These people of ours have taken gods besides Him. Why do they not bring clear proof for them? Who, then, is more unjust than one who fabricates a lie against Allah?'} Allah, Mighty and Majestic, praised them for rejecting what their people said, since their people had not established clear proof for their claim; and He affirmed as true their statement that whoever claims ...


[Page 19]

A statement without evidence is a fabrication of lies against Allah, Mighty and Majestic.

And He, Exalted is He, said: {Rather, those who do wrong follow their desires without knowledge. So who can guide those whom Allah has led astray? They will have no helpers.} Thus, as you hear, He, Exalted is He, declared that whoever follows a statement and agrees with it without knowing that it is sound is a wrongdoer, and that whoever does not return to the truth he hears is among the people of the Fire. And He, Exalted is He, said: {If they do not respond to you, then know that they only follow their desires. And who is more astray than one who follows his desire without guidance from Allah? Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.}
Allah, Exalted is He, condemned a person for denying what he does not know, saying: {Rather, they denied what they had not encompassed in knowledge, and whose interpretation had not yet come to them. Thus did those before them deny; so see what was the end of the wrongdoers.} Thus, all that we have mentioned establishes the duty to refrain from what we do not know and to return to what the proof requires once it has been established. Allah, Exalted is He, also said: {And who is more unjust than one who invents a lie about Allah or denies the truth when it has come to him? Is there not in Hell a dwelling for the disbelievers?} Abu Muhammad said: This verse is sufficient to establish that no one should affirm anything for which no proof has been established, nor do anything that the proof has established. Who is more unjust than one who knows what we have mentioned, yet follows a whisper arising within himself, or a report for which no proof has been established that it must be believed, or blindly follows a person like himself, who may, in the sight of Allah, Exalted is He, be mistaken in what he supposes? In any case, that person is not infallible: he may err or be right. Allah, Exalted is He, also said: {And they said, "None will enter Paradise unless he is a Jew or a Christian." Those are their wishes. Say, "Produce your proof, if you are truthful."} Thus, Allah, Exalted is He, required that whoever is truthful in his claim produce proof; and if he does not produce it,
On the basis of proof, he is a liar and a falsifier, or an ignoramus. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Here you are, disputing about that of which you have knowledge; why then do you dispute about that of which you have no knowledge? Allah knows, and you do not know.} Thus, He, Exalted is He, permitted disputation only on the basis of knowledge and prohibited it without knowledge. } ))
From the foregoing, it is clear that there is a difference between a believer whose faith is general and who suspends judgment about what he does not know, and one who fabricates lies against Allah and speaks without knowledge. The ruling on the latter is different. There is no room here for the objection that a person does not have enough time to learn all the sciences, because we say to him: Suspend judgment, do not fabricate lies against Allah, and say, "I believe in everything that has come from Allah"; otherwise, you will be punished as a fabricator. Allah called those who brought three upright, just witnesses to a crime of adultery, but were unable to bring a fourth, liars. He, Exalted is He, said: ((Why did they not produce four witnesses against it? Since they did not produce the witnesses, then in the sight of Allah they are the liars.)) Their backs are thus made liable to be flogged; indeed, the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) flogged Mistah al-Badri, whom Allah had forgiven, for this very reason: slandering a woman—any woman, even if she were the most immoral of the Muslims, so long as she kept her chastity and had not committed adultery.
It is stated in "Al-Fasl fi al-Milal wa al-Ahwa wa al-Nihal" (3/140):
"Abu Muhammad said: We say to anyone who declares a person an unbeliever merely for his statement, before proof has been established against him and he has defied the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him), finding distress in himself over what he brought: Tell us, did the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) leave out anything of Islam such that whoever does not affirm it is an unbeliever, except ..."


[Page 20]

He made it clear and called all people to it, so there must be agreement. Whoever denies this is an unbeliever, without disagreement. Once he acknowledges that, he is asked: Did it ever come from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) that he refused to accept the faith of the people of a village or a locality, or of any person who came to him, free or enslaved, man or woman, unless they first affirmed that ability precedes the act or accompanies it, that the Quran is uncreated, that Allah, Exalted is He, will be seen or will not be seen, or that He has hearing, sight, life, or any of the other superfluous matters of the theologians, which Satan introduced among them to sow enmity and hatred between them? If he claims that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) never invited anyone to Islam without first explaining these matters to them, he has lied, by the consensus of the Muslims on earth, and said what he knows to be false. He has also claimed that all the Companions (may Allah be pleased with them) colluded to conceal this aspect of the Prophet's conduct. This is naturally impossible; moreover, it imputes unbelief to them, since they concealed something without which no one's Islam could be complete. If they say that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) never invited anyone to any of these matters, but that they are contained in the Quran and in his words, it is said to them: You have spoken truly. This confirms that if ignorance of any of all these matters were unbelief, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) would not have neglected to explain them to free and enslaved men and women. Whoever considers this possible has said that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) did not convey the message as he was commanded to do; this is outright unbelief on the part of whoever permits it. It is therefore necessarily established that ignorance of all this does no harm at all. Discussion of these matters becomes obligatory only when people engage in them; then it is obligatory to clarify the truth from the Quran and Sunnah, in accordance with the words of Allah, Mighty and Majestic is He: {Be steadfast for Allah, bearing witness with justice}, and His words, Mighty and Majestic is He: {You shall make it clear to the people and not conceal it}. Whoever, after the truth has been clarified, stubbornly persists is an unbeliever, because he has not accepted the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) as judge, nor submitted to his ruling. It is authentically reported from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) that a man who had never done any good said, when death came to him, to his family: "When I die, burn me, then scatter my ashes on a windy day, half in the sea and half on land. By Allah, if Allah, Exalted is He, has power over me, He will punish me with a punishment unlike that of anyone among His creation." Allah, Mighty and Majestic is He, gathered his ashes and brought him back to life, then asked him, "What made you do that?" He replied, "Fear of You, my Lord." And Allah, Exalted is He, forgave him for this statement.
Abu Muhammad said: "This was a man who remained ignorant until he died that Allah, Mighty and Majestic is He, is able to gather his ashes and bring him back to life. Yet He forgave him because of his acknowledgment, his fear, and his ignorance."
This passage makes clear the difference between someone who believes in a general way, then remains silent and submits, without fabricating lies about Allah, and someone who does not remain silent, but instead fabricates lies about Allah by asserting some form of polytheism, unbelief, or sin without proof, and falls short in seeking knowledge of the matter. The discussion of this latter type will come later, in a passage from Ibn Hazm, God willing.
Here are some quotations from scholars showing that one who is negligent in seeking knowledge and speaks about Allah without knowledge is not excused. Some of them also show that not being excused does not mean that he is punished only for his negligence in seeking knowledge; rather, he is punished for the act he himself committed. According to the position of the scholars cited below, he is punished for the very forbidden act or act of polytheism, not merely for falling short in


[Page 21]

Seeking knowledge only ((Here is a selection of statements by scholars explaining the criterion for being able to learn:
Some of them will mention circumstances in which ignorance is excused and those in which it is not, as examples of being able or unable to learn. These are the circumstances I mentioned above.
1 - Abu Muhammad ibn Hazm, may Allah have mercy on him
He said: "I have seen people who hold that the religious laws do not bind someone who is ignorant of them, or someone to whom they have not been conveyed."
Abu Muhammad said: "This is false. Rather, they do bind him, because the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) was sent to all human beings, all jinn, and everyone not yet born, once they reach maturity after birth."
(Ability to learn) Abu Muhammad said: "Allah Most High said, commanding him to say: {I am the Messenger of Allah to you all} (al-A'raf: 158). This is a general statement from which no one may be excluded. And He Most High said: {Does man think that he will be left neglected?} (al-Qiyama: 36). Thus, Exalted is He, denied that anyone could be left neglected. The neglected person is one who is left without being commanded or forbidden. He, Mighty and Majestic, denied that this could be so. But a person is excused for his ignorance and for being unable to attain knowledge alone. As for someone to whom news of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) has reached, wherever he may be, even in the farthest reaches of the earth, it is incumbent upon him to seek him out. Once his warning has reached him, it is incumbent upon him to believe in him and follow him, seek the religion that is binding upon him, and leave his homeland for that purpose. Otherwise, he has incurred disbelief, everlasting residence in the Fire, and punishment, as stated explicitly in the Quran. Everything we have mentioned refutes the claim of those Kharijites who say that at the time of the sending of the...


[Page 22]

The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) obligates those in the farthest reaches of the earth to believe in him and learn his laws. If they die in that state, they die as unbelievers and go to the Fire. This contradicts the words of Allah, Exalted is He: {Allah does not burden any soul beyond its capacity. It will have what it has earned, and it will bear what it has acquired} (al-Baqara: 286), for no one is capable of knowing the unseen.
If they say, "This is the proof of the group who hold that no one is bound by any of the religious laws until they reach him," we say: They have no proof in this, because everything people are charged with is within their capacity and what their constitution can bear. However, they are excused because it has not reached them, and they have not been charged with it in a way that would bring punishment if they failed to do it. Rather, they have been charged with it in a way that does not bring punishment until it reaches them. "Whoever learns from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) that a ruling has been prescribed for him, whether in general terms or without its specific text reaching him, is obliged to exert himself in seeking that ruling; otherwise, he is disobedient to Allah, Exalted is He. Allah, Exalted is He, says: {So ask the people of knowledge if you do not know} (al-Nahl: 43)." (1)
Ibn Hazm also said: "Allah, Exalted is He, says: {We would never punish until We had sent a messenger} (al-Isra: 15). Thus it is established that no unbeliever is punished at all until the warning of the Messenger (peace and blessings be upon him) reaches him. As for one to whom mention of the Prophet Muhammad (peace and blessings be upon him) and what he brought has reached, but who can find no one in his land to tell him about it, he is obliged to leave it for a land where he can investigate the facts. Were it not for his statement, peace be upon him, that there is no prophet after him, we would be obliged to do this with everyone we hear claiming prophethood." Then he said: "Anyone among us who is in a remote place where he can find no one to teach him the laws of his religion, whether man or woman, is obliged to travel to a place where they can find ..."


[Page 23]

a jurist to teach them their religion, or that they be sent a jurist to teach them the affairs of their religion. If the imam knows this, let him send them a jurist to teach them] (And said
((Ali, Allah Most High has enjoined upon every group that it warn its people, and has enjoined upon their people that they accept its warning, in His saying, Exalted is He: {The believers should not all go forth. Why, then, should not a group from every faction go forth, so that they may gain understanding of the religion and warn their people when they return to them, so that they may beware?} He has thus warned against opposing the warning of a group. In Arabic, the word "group" applies to a part of something, as we have explained. No two Muslims would disagree that if a trustworthy Muslim entered the land of unbelief, called a people to Islam, recited the Quran to them, and taught them the religious laws, they would be obliged to accept it, and proof would thereby have been established against them. The same would apply if the caliph or an emir sent a messenger to a king among the unbelievers or to a nation among the unbelievers.
He called them to Islam and taught them the Quran and the laws of religion. There is no difference, and no Muslim has ever said that the people of Yemen were required to say to Muadh and to those whom the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) sent to every region as teachers, jurists, and Quran reciters: "Yes, you are the Messenger of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him)," and that the pledge of faith was binding.
We have, however, what you have given us as a legal opinion and taught us of the rulings of prayer, the cases arising in zakat, and the rest of the religion, from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), and what you have taught us of the Qur'an from him, peace be upon him: we will neither accept it from you nor take it from you, because lying is possible and conceivable on your part, until there comes to us, for all of that, sufficient corroboration and mass transmission. Indeed, if they were to say that


[Page 24]

they would be non-Muslims. Nor do two people differ over the fact that the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) sent those of his messengers whom he sent to distant lands only so that they might convey to their people the Quran, the Sunnah, and the laws of religion; he did not send them to institute for them a religion that he himself had not brought from Allah, Exalted is He. It is therefore established by all this that everything reliably transmitted by one trustworthy person from another, reaching back to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him), whether from the Quran or the Sunnah, must be accepted, acknowledged, affirmed, believed in, and followed as religion. And anything reliably reported from a Companion, a Successor, or anyone after them, whether a recitation not traced back to the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), or a legal opinion not traced back to him (peace and blessings be upon him), may not be accepted, for neither Allah, Exalted is He, nor His Messenger (peace and blessings be upon him) made it binding. All such reports have indeed been authentically transmitted from individual Companions and Successors. Their virtue does not make it obligatory to accept their opinions, nor does it preclude the possibility that they may have erred in what they said based on their own judgment. Rather, their virtue covers every error they made and outweighs it, and entails that they be revered and loved. And Allah, Exalted is He, is the source of success. Another proof is that it has been established with certainty and is known necessarily that all the Companions, from the first to the last, agreed, with none of them dissenting, nor any of the Successors who lived in their time, that whenever any one of them faced a new case, he would ask a Companion about it and follow his opinion. They would ask him only about what the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) had made binding in religion from Allah, Exalted is He, concerning that particular case; no one ever asked any person …
Among them was the introduction into religion of a law that Allah Most High had not authorized. The same applies to every generation after them, generation after generation; we exclude no one. There is absolutely no disagreement between believer and unbeliever that


[Page 25]

Every Companion and Successor who was asked by someone seeking a legal ruling about a religious matter never once told him, "You may not act on what I have told you from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) until the soothsayers tell you the same," as they said to them regarding what they had reported seeing from them, without requiring them to accept it. If it is said, "Then make this very argument proof for accepting a mursal report," we say: That is not so, for there was never any consensus, either in early times or later, on accepting a mursal report. Among the Successors were those who did not accept it, such as al-Zuhri and others. They would ask those who informed them who had informed them, until they traced it back to the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him).
That requirement was waived only for those who lacked the ability to understand and know the chain of transmission. Al-Zuhri said to the people of Syria, "Why do I see that your hadiths have neither halters nor reins?" They then followed his instruction. There were many others besides al-Zuhri. Thus it is established that the entire Muslim community agreed to accept the report of a trustworthy individual from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him). Every group followed this in its own learning, including the People of the Sunnah, the Kharijites, the Shi'a, and the Qadariyya, until the Mu'tazilite theologians arose after the first century and opposed the consensus on this matter."
He said: "Allah, Exalted is He, praised the Companions of the Cave: {We relate to you their story in truth. They were youths who believed in their Lord, and We increased them in guidance. 13 And We strengthened their hearts when they stood up and said, 'Our Lord is the Lord of the heavens and the earth. We will never call upon any god besides Him; if we did, we would indeed have spoken outrageously. 14 These...}


[Page 26]

"Our people have taken gods besides Him. Why do they not produce clear proof for them? Who is more unjust than one who fabricates a lie against Allah?" Thus Allah, Mighty and Majestic, praised them for rejecting what their people said, since their people had no clear proof for their claim, and He affirmed as true their statement that whoever claims...
A statement without evidence is a lie fabricated against Allah, Mighty and Majestic.

Allah, Exalted is He, says: {And who is more unjust than one who is reminded of the signs of his Lord, then turns away from them? Indeed, We will take retribution from the criminals.} No one is more unjust than one against whom proof has been established from the Book of Allah, Exalted is He, and the words of His Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), yet turns away from it, though it is the decisive proof and the clear argument. Allah, Exalted is He, says: {Those who consume usury will not stand except as one stands who is being beaten by Satan into madness. That is because they say, "Trade is just like usury." But Allah has permitted trade and forbidden usury. So whoever receives an admonition from his Lord and desists may keep what is past, and his affair rests with Allah. But whoever returns to it—those are the companions of the Fire; they will abide therein forever.} And He, Exalted is He, says: {Rather, those who do wrong follow their desires without knowledge. So who can guide one whom Allah has led astray? They will have no helpers.} Thus, as you hear, He, Exalted is He, has informed us that whoever follows a statement that accords with his view without knowing that it is sound is an unjust person, and that whoever does not return to the truth he hears is among the people of the Fire. Allah, Exalted is He, says: {If they do not respond to you, then know that they only follow their desires. And who is more astray than one who follows his desire without guidance from Allah? Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.} Allah, Exalted is He, condemned a person for denying what he does not know, saying: {Rather, they have denied what they have not comprehended...}


[Page 27]

By His knowledge, while its interpretation has not yet come to them. Likewise did those before them deny the truth. So see what was the end of the wrongdoers." Thus, everything we have mentioned makes it clear that one must refrain from what one does not know and return to what the proof requires once it has been established. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Who is more unjust than one who fabricates a lie against Allah or denies the truth when it comes to him? Is there not in Hell a dwelling for the disbelievers?} Abu Muhammad said: This verse is sufficient to establish that no one should affirm anything for which no proof has been established, nor do anything for which proof has been established. Who is more unjust than one who knows what we have mentioned, yet follows a whisper arising within himself, or a report for which no proof has established that it must be believed, or imitates a person like himself, who may, in the sight of Allah, be mistaken in what he supposes? In any case, he is protected from error, yet he may be right or wrong. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {And they say, "None shall enter Paradise unless he is a Jew or a Christian." Those are their wishful fancies. Say, "Bring your proof, if you are truthful."} Thus Allah required that whoever is truthful in his claim must produce proof; and if he does not produce it,
By proof, he is a liar and a falsifier, or an ignorant person. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Here you are, disputing about that of which you have knowledge; why then do you dispute about that of which you have no knowledge? Allah knows, and you do not know.} Thus, He, Exalted is He, permitted disputation only on the basis of knowledge and prohibited it without knowledge.
And the Exalted said: {They will say, "They were three, and their dog was the fourth"; and they will say, "They were five, and their dog was the sixth," guessing at the unseen; and they will say, "They were seven, and their dog was the eighth." Say, "My Lord knows best their number. None knows them except a few." So do not argue about them except with a clear argument, and do not ask any of them about them.} Abu Muhammad said: When we found that Allah, Exalted is He, had commanded in the verses we mentioned


[Page 28]

through argumentation and disputation, and required the acceptance of nothing except on the basis of proof, we were obliged to seek out the blameworthy disputation, as we have explained. We found that He said: {We send the messengers only as bearers of glad tidings and warners. But those who disbelieve dispute with falsehood in order to refute the truth by it, and they take My signs and the warnings given to them in mockery.} Thus, as you see, Exalted is He condemned disputation without proof and disputation in support of falsehood. By this, Exalted is He refuted the claim of the deluded: "Every person who is led astray is furnished with an argument." Exalted is He made clear that the deluded person is one who is not furnished with an argument, whereas the one who is right is, in truth, furnished with an argument; they are the people of truth. And Exalted is He said: {Those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah without any authority having come to them—it is greatly hateful in the sight of Allah and in the sight of those who believe. Thus does Allah seal the heart of every arrogant tyrant.} These verses together clarify both blameworthy disputation and praiseworthy, obligatory disputation. The obligatory kind is that in which the person disputing seeks to make the truth manifest. The blameworthy kind has two forms, as stated in the verses we have cited: first, disputing without knowledge; and second, disputing in support of falsehood through sophistry and deception after the truth has become clear to him. This makes clear that the truth is one, and that nothing is valid except what is established by rational proof. These are the blameworthy people of whom Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Have you not seen those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah? How are they turned away?} And His saying, Exalted is He: {Among the people are those who dispute concerning Allah without knowledge and follow every rebellious devil.} And His saying, Exalted is He: {Among the people are those who dispute concerning Allah without knowledge, guidance, or an illuminating Book, turning his neck aside to lead others astray from the path of Allah. For him there is disgrace in this world, and on the Day of Resurrection We shall make him taste the punishment of the Blaze.}
Ibn Taymiyya (may Allah have mercy on him) said: "The ruling of a divine address is not established with respect to those legally responsible until they are able to know it; this is the soundest of the opinions." (3).


[Page 29]

He also said: [Allah says, "We would never punish until We had sent a messenger" (al-Isra: 15). The proof against people is established only on two conditions: that they are able to know what Allah has revealed, and that they are able to act upon it. As for one who is unable to know, such as an insane person, or unable to act, there is no command or prohibition for him. If knowledge of part of the religion is cut off, or one is unable to observe part of it, then for the person unable to know or act, that is like being cut off from knowledge of the entire religion or being unable to observe all of it, as in the case of an insane person, for example. These are times of interruption.] (4).
Ibn Taymiyya also said: [If this is clear, then whoever abandons some obligatory aspect of faith because he is unable to fulfill it, either because he is unable to acquire knowledge—for example, the message has not reached him—or because he is unable to act, is not commanded to do what he is incapable of, and that is not part of the obligatory faith and religion incumbent upon him, even though it is part of the religion and faith that are obligatory in principle.] (5).
He also said: [If religious obligation is conditional upon the ability to acquire knowledge, whose basis is reason, and upon the ability to act] (6).
(And Ibn Taymiyya also said: [This excuse is valid only when one is unable to remove it; otherwise, whenever a person is able to know the truth but falls short in doing so, he is not excused.] (7).
He also said: [In short, there is no disagreement among the Muslims that someone who is in a land of unbelief, has believed, and is unable to emigrate is not required to observe religious duties that he is incapable of fulfilling; rather, the obligation is according to one's ability. The same applies to what one does not know the ruling of. If, for example, he did not know that


[Page 30]

Prayer was obligatory upon him, yet he remained for a period without praying. He was not required to make up the prayers, according to the more apparent of the two opinions of the scholars. This is the position of Abu Hanifa and the literalists, and one of the two positions in the school of Ahmad—until he said: "The correct view in all of this is that a ruling is established only when one is able to know it, and that he is not required to make up what he did not know was obligatory." (8)
(The following statement is explicit that he is subject to the threat for committing what is forbidden, and that the matter is not merely the sin of his negligence in seeking knowledge.) He said: "This is because the threat applies to one who commits what is forbidden only if he knows that it is forbidden, or is able to know that it is forbidden. For someone raised in the desert, or someone newly entered into Islam, who does something forbidden without knowing that it is forbidden, does not sin and is not subject to the prescribed punishment, even if he has no legal evidence on which to base his belief that it is permissible. One who has not received the hadith prohibiting something, but relies on legal evidence in holding it permissible, is all the more deserving of being excused." Ibn Taymiyya also said: "As for one who deliberately distorts the Book, in its wording or meaning, or who knows what the Messenger brought and opposes it, such a person deserves punishment. The same applies to one who is negligent in seeking and following the truth, following his own desires and occupying himself with worldly affairs instead." (5)
Ibn al-Qayyim's words on the same issue.
(Being able to know is sufficient for the proof to be established.) He, may Allah have mercy on him, said: "A servant's acknowledgment that Allah's proof has been established against him is among the requirements of faith, whether he obeys or disobeys. For Allah's proof has been established against the servant.final


[Page 31]

Through the sending of the Messenger, the revelation of the Book, its reaching him, and his ability to learn of it—whether he knows or is ignorant. Everyone who is able to learn what Allah has commanded and forbidden, but falls short and does not learn it, has had the proof established against him.] (1).
(Punishment is for the act, not for falling short) He also said: [If it is asked: Is this person excused for his misguidance if he thinks he is rightly guided, as Allah, Exalted is He, says: {And they think that they are rightly guided} (al-A'raf: 30)? The answer is: No. This person and others like him who are misguided have no excuse, for their misguidance stems from turning away from the revelation brought by the Messenger (peace and blessings be upon him). Even if he thinks he is rightly guided, he has fallen short by turning away from following the caller to guidance. If he goes astray, it is only because of his own negligence and turning away. This is unlike one whose misguidance is due to the message not reaching him and his inability to access it; such a person has a different ruling. The warnings in the Qur'an apply only to the former. As for the latter, Allah does not punish anyone until after the proof has been established against him.] (4).
(Punishment is for the act, not for falling short) Ibn al-Qayyim also said: [It is authentically reported from the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) that he said, "Whoever calls to misguidance will bear a sin like the sins of those who follow him, without that diminishing their sins in the least." This indicates that the disbelief of those who follow them is due simply to following and imitating them. Yes, this matter must be explained in detail to resolve the difficulty: the distinction is between an imitator who is able to learn and know the truth but turns away from it, and an imitator who is in no way able to do so. Both kinds exist. The one who is able but turns away is negligent and has abandoned what is incumbent upon him; he has no excuse before Allah. As for one who is unable to ask or learn, who does not


[Page 32]

Those who are capable of understanding the truth are also of two kinds. The first seeks guidance, prefers and loves it, but is unable to attain it or seek it because there is no one to guide him. His case is like that of those who lived in an interval between prophets and those to whom the message did not reach. The second turns away, has no desire for guidance, and does not even consider anything other than the way he is on. The first says, "My Lord, if I knew of a religion better for You than the one I follow, I would follow it and abandon what I am on. But I know nothing other than what I am on, and I am unable to do anything else. This is the utmost of my effort and the limit of my knowledge." The second is content with what he is on, prefers nothing else to it, and seeks nothing beyond it. To him, there is no difference between being unable and being able; both are equally beyond his reach. The latter should not be classed with the former, given the difference between them: the first is like someone who sought the religion during an interval between prophets but did not find it, and so turned away from it after exhausting every effort to seek it, out of inability and ignorance; the second is like someone who did not seek it at all, but died in his polytheism, even though he would have been unable to attain it had he sought it. There is a difference, then, between the inability of one who seeks and the inability of one who turns away. Reflect on this point. Allah will judge His servants on the Day of Resurrection with His judgment and justice, and He will punish only those against whom His proof has been established through the messengers. This is certain with regard to people in general. As for whether the proof was established against a particular person, Zayd or Amr, that is not something one can intrude upon between Allah and His servants. Rather, a servant must believe that everyone who follows a religion other than Islam is an unbeliever, and that Allah, Exalted is He, does not punish anyone until the proof has been established against him through a messenger. This is the general rule; determining individual cases is entrusted to Allah's knowledge and judgment. This concerns the rulings on reward and punishment. As for the rulings of this world, they are


[Page 33]

It follows the apparent meaning of the ruling: the children and the insane among the unbelievers are unbelievers in the rulings of this world, and are treated like their guardians. With this distinction, the difficulty in the matter is resolved.] (1).
Al-Qarafi, may Allah have mercy on him, said: ["The ninety-fourth distinction: between the principle of what ignorance is not an excuse for and the principle of what ignorance is an excuse for." Know that the Lawgiver has been lenient regarding certain forms of ignorance in the Sharia, pardoning those who commit them, while in other cases He has taken people to account for their ignorance and has not pardoned those who commit them. The criterion for the forms of ignorance that are pardoned is that they are ones that it is ordinarily impossible to guard against. As for what it is possible to guard against without hardship, it is not pardoned.] (2).
Al-Qarafi also said: [The principle of the Sharia indicates that any ignorance which a legally responsible person can dispel is no proof for the ignorant person. For Allah, Exalted is He, sent His messengers to His creation with His messages and made it obligatory upon all of them to learn them and then act upon them. Thus, knowledge and acting upon it are obligations. (Punishment is for the act, not merely for negligence.) Whoever neglects learning and acting, and remains ignorant, has committed two acts of disobedience by neglecting two obligations.
