When it was part of the night, he looked, but could not distinguish them. When morning came, he said, "Messenger of Allah, I put two cords beneath my pillow." He said, "Your pillow must be very wide indeed, if the white thread and the black thread were beneath it."
Here, Adi ibn Hatim interpreted the verse incorrectly and thought that Allah had prescribed this ruling in this manner: that one should begin fasting when the two cords' white and black colors became distinguishable. This was a matter of belief, and an attribution to Allah of something He certainly did not intend. Nevertheless, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) did not declare him a disbeliever; rather, he excused him for following the apparent meaning of the wording in the language. This applies to all cases of error in interpreting the Quran, since the …


[Page 211]

Every interpreter of a verse who attributes to Allah an active attribute—that He intends such and such by this verse—and anyone who rejects this interpretation and adopts another is denying this attribute of Allah while affirming a different one for Him. One of the two must be mistaken, if the two interpretations cannot be reconciled. According to this view, those who declare interpretation to be disbelief would have to declare most interpreters disbelievers. And Allah knows best. If it is said, "Why did you not cite a practical ruling concerning fasting in the matter of being excused on account of interpretation?" we say what we said before: affirming a practical ruling, according to your classification, entails several matters of belief. These are that Allah intended this ruling and spoke it with His saying, "Be," which affirms a specific active attribute; that Allah then recorded it in the Preserved Tablet; that Allah then sent Gabriel with it to the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him); and that Allah will punish or reward whoever does this thing. All of these are matters of belief from which they cannot extricate themselves, and they apply to every issue relating to actions that we will cite as evidence.
– Among the proofs is the report of Ammar: "The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) sent me on an errand, and I became junub but could not find water. So I rolled in the dust as an animal rolls, then I came to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) and told him about it. He said, 'It would have sufficed for you to do this with your hands.' Then he struck the ground once with his hands, wiped his left hand over his right, and wiped the backs of his hands and his face." And in it is the Messenger's (peace and blessings be upon him) excusing Ammar in his interpretation of the verse on tayammum. There is no doubt that this interpretation attributes this ruling to Allah in his interpretation of the verse on tayammum.
– Umar's statement about Hatib: "He has disbelieved."


[Page 212]

Imam al-Bazzar, may Allah have mercy on him, said, as recorded in Kashf al-Astar (vol. 3, p. 255): Muhammad ibn al-Muthanna narrated to us, saying: Umar ibn Yunus narrated to us, saying: Ikrima ibn Ammar narrated to us, saying: Abu Zumayl narrated to us, saying: Ibn Abbas narrated to us, saying: I heard Umar ibn al-Khattab say: Hatib ibn Abi Balta'a wrote a letter to Mecca, and Allah informed His Prophet of it. He sent Ali and al-Zubayr after the letter. They caught up with a woman riding a camel and took it out from one of her braids. The Prophet of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said nothing to them. He then sent for Hatib and said, "Hatib, did you write this letter?" He replied, "Yes, Messenger of Allah." He said, "What made you do that?" Hatib replied, "By Allah, I am sincere toward Allah and His Messenger. But I was a stranger among the people of Mecca, while my family were living among them, and I feared for them. So I wrote a letter that would not harm Allah or His Messenger in any way, hoping it might benefit my family." Umar said: I drew my sword and said, "Messenger of Allah, let me deal with Hatib, for he has disbelieved; let me strike off his head." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said, "Ibn al-Khattab, how do you know? Perhaps Allah looked upon this company of the people of Badr and said, 'Do as you wish, for I have forgiven you.'"
Al-Bazzar said: The story of Hatib has been reported through more than one chain.
Abu Abd al-Rahman said: This is a hasan hadith. The hadith shows that Umar is excused for his interpretation, since he judged Hatib to have disbelieved based on an interpretation.


[Page 213]

-Among the proofs is Khalid's killing of Banu Jadhima, thinking that they remained in disbelief—the hadith is as follows.
Mahmud narrated to me: Abd al-Razzaq narrated to us: Ma'mar informed us. And Nu'aym narrated to me: Abdullah informed us: Ma'mar informed us, from al-Zuhri, from Salim, from his father, who said: The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) sent Khalid ibn al-Walid to Banu Jadhimah and called them to Islam. They could not properly say, "We have submitted," so they kept saying, "We have become Sabaeans, we have become Sabaeans." Khalid began killing some of them and taking others captive, and he assigned each of us a captive. Then, one day, Khalid ordered each of us to kill his captive. I said, "By Allah, I will not kill my captive, and none of my companions will kill his captive," until we came to the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) and told him about it. The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) raised his hands and said, "O Allah, I disavow before You what Khalid has done," twice.
[Sh: (Banu Jadhima) was one of the Arab tribes. (We have left our religion) We have moved from one religion to another. They intended to enter Islam, but Khalid (may Allah be pleased with him) thought that they had not submitted, since they had not said, "We have embraced Islam." (I disavow myself before You) I apologize. (For what Khalid did) in killing and taking these people captive.] Here Khalid declared unbelievers those who did not deserve to be declared unbelievers, acting on his own interpretation. [6766]
Khalid's killing of those from Khath'am who sought protection through prostration, and his not being satisfied with the Islamic profession as a basis for judging them to be Muslims. This is a hadith narrated from Jarir ibn Abd Allah al-Bajali, who said: "The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) sent a detachment to Khath'am.


[Page 214]

Some of them sought protection by prostrating, but the killing among them was swift. This reached the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), and he ordered that half the blood money be paid for them. He said, "I disavow every Muslim who lives among the polytheists." They said, "O Messenger of Allah, their fires should not be visible to one another." Here, the Companions assumed that these people had remained disbelievers, based on their interpretation, so the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) paid blood money for them and did not declare those who had killed them disbelievers.

Another example is the hadith, "None of you should pray the afternoon prayer except at Banu Qurayza." It appears in Sahih al-Bukhari (5/112):

Abd Allah ibn Muhammad ibn Asma narrated to us: Juwayriya ibn Asma narrated to us, from Nafi, from Ibn Umar (may Allah be pleased with them both), who said: The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said on the day of the Confederates, "None of you should pray the afternoon prayer except at Banu Qurayza." Some of them caught the afternoon prayer while on the way. Some said, "We will not pray until we reach it," while others said, "Rather, we will pray; he did not mean for us to do that." This was mentioned to the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), and he did not rebuke any of them.

The Companions differed on a matter of creed, namely whether the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) saw Allah. Most of the Companions held that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) did not see Allah, Exalted is He, with his eyes on the Night Journey and Ascension.

It is authentically reported from Aisha (may Allah be pleased with her) that she said, "Whoever tells you that Muhammad (peace and blessings be upon him) saw his Lord has lied," and she would say, "Sight cannot perceive Him..." Narrated by al-Bukhari (Tawhid/6832).


[Page 215]

Abu Dharr reported: I asked the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him), "Have you seen your Lord?" He replied, "There is light; how could I see Him?" Narrated by Muslim (Faith/261).

Ibn Abbas reported: "The heart did not deny what it saw. And he certainly saw him another time." He said, "He saw him with his heart twice." Narrated by Muslim (Faith/258).

Furthermore, one of the Companions asked the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), "How can knowledge be taken away when the Book of Allah is among us?" Yet he did not declare him an unbeliever. Aisha, interpreting the matter, thought that the believer who is saved from the Fire would be called to account on the Day of Resurrection; this is a matter of belief, yet he did not declare her an unbeliever. He explained to her that his deeds would merely be presented to him. The hadith is in al-Bukhari. Hafsa thought that those who had pledged allegiance beneath the tree would enter the Fire, interpreting the words of the Exalted, "There is none of you who will not pass over it." This is a matter of belief, yet he did not declare her an unbeliever. The hadith is in Musnad Ahmad, and it is authentic. A woman interpreted the matter as permitting a woman with irregular bleeding to leave the prayer; leaving the prayer is unbelief according to the majority of the Companions, because she thought that irregular bleeding was menstruation. In a hadith, Abu Dawud (may Allah have mercy on him) said (vol. 1, p. 488): Wahb ibn Baqiyya narrated to us; Khalid narrated to us, from Suhayl—that is, Ibn Abi Salih—from al-Zuhri, from Urwa ibn al-Zubayr, from Asma bint Umays, who said: I said, "Messenger of Allah, Fatima bint Abi Hubays has had irregular bleeding for such-and-such a time and has not prayed." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said, "Glory be to Allah! This is indeed from…"


[Page 216]

Satan, so that she may sit in a basin. When she sees yellow discharge above the water, let her perform one ritual bath for the noon and afternoon prayers, one ritual bath for the sunset and night prayers, and one ritual bath for the dawn prayer, and perform ablution in between."
This is a sound hadith according to the conditions of Muslim. A woman experienced a menstrual period that prevented her from fasting and praying. This is so, even though Aisha asked the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), "Does Allah know everything that people conceal?" He did not declare her an unbeliever; the hadith is in Sahih Muslim. A man also interpreted the law to mean that the prescribed punishment for fornication would be waived for his unmarried son if he paid a ransom of one hundred sheep and a slave girl. The man was not declared an unbeliever; the hadith is in al-Bukhari. A man was unaware that wine was forbidden and brought it as a gift to the Messenger of Allah. This involved deeming something forbidden to be permissible, yet he was not declared an unbeliever; the hadith is in Muslim. Abu Hurayra did not know who would be the happiest of people to receive the Prophet's intercession (peace and blessings be upon him), so the Prophet explained to him that it would be the one who said, "There is no god but Allah," sincerely from his heart; Abu Hurayra was not declared an unbeliever. The Companions did not know that they would be rewarded for sexual intercourse, and they asked, "Does one of us satisfy his desire and receive a reward for it?" The hadith is established in the Sahih collections. Aisha did not know that all the deeds of an unbeliever are rendered void. She thought that he might benefit in the Hereafter from some of his good deeds, and asked about Ibn Jud'an, saying that he used to feed guests and maintain ties of kinship: "Will that benefit him?" The Prophet explained to her that it would not, but did not declare her an unbeliever. The hadith is established in the Sahih collections. This is a matter of creed. Al-Abbas did not know that Abu Talib's punishment would be lightened because he had protected the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him). This too is a matter of creed, yet he was not declared an unbeliever. It is also reported that Imam Ahmad, may Allah have mercy on him, said (vol. 3, p. 478): Ibn Abi Adi narrated to us, from Dawud ibn Abi Hind.


[Page 217]

Al-Sha'bi, from Alqama, from Salama ibn Yazid al-Ju'fi, who said: "My brother and I went to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him). We said, 'Messenger of Allah, our mother, Mulayka, used to maintain ties of kinship, honor guests, and do such-and-such. She died in the pre-Islamic era. Will that benefit her in any way?' He said, 'No.' We said, 'She also buried a sister of ours alive in the pre-Islamic era. Will that benefit her in any way?' He said, 'The woman who buries a child alive and the child who is buried alive are in the Fire, unless the woman who buried the child alive lives to see Islam, in which case Allah will forgive her.'"
This is an authentic hadith; its narrators are among the narrators of the Sahih collections. These people were ignorant of the ruling concerning the unbeliever in the Hereafter, which is a matter of belief, yet he did not declare them unbelievers. A man was ignorant of the fate of his unbelieving father, who had died in the pre-Islamic era, and asked the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) about him, "Where is my father?" He did not declare him an unbeliever; rather, he told him that his father was in the Fire. This is a matter of belief, and the hadith is in Sahih Muslim. A man was ignorant of the nature of jihad. Abu Musa reported: The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about a man who fights out of courage, one who fights out of zeal, and one who fights to be seen by others: "Which of these is in the path of Allah?" The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) replied, "Whoever fights so that the word of Allah may be supreme is in the path of Allah." This is a matter of belief, yet he did not declare him an unbeliever. A woman was ignorant that she had to perform ghusl after experiencing a wet dream, yet he did not declare her an unbeliever; the hadith is in al-Bukhari. The Companions were ignorant that the spoils had to be divided before the animals were slaughtered, so they slaughtered the sheep before they were divided, yet he did not declare them unbelievers; the hadith is in al-Bukhari. The Companions were ignorant of where people would be when the earth was changed into another earth, and the heavens likewise, so he explained to them that they would be in darkness, before the Bridge. This is a matter of belief, yet he did not declare them unbelievers. A group interpreted...


[Page 218]

Among the Companions, concerning the hypocrites, Allah did not declare the group that interpreted the matter to be unbelievers, in His saying, {What is the matter with you that you have become two groups concerning the hypocrites, when Allah has cast them back because of what they have earned? Do you wish to guide those whom Allah has led astray? Whoever Allah leads astray, you will never find a way for him (88).} It says in al-Tahrir wa al-Tanwir (5/149):
The interrogative is for expressing astonishment and reproach. The definite article in "the hypocrites" is for reference to those already known, and "two groups" is an adverbial state referring to the pronoun governed by the preposition "for"; thus it qualifies the element governing it, namely the reproach. It is therefore clear that the object of reproach is their division. "Concerning the hypocrites" is connected to "two groups," which is interpreted to mean "divided"; its meaning is "concerning the matter of the hypocrites," since a ruling does not pertain to the persons of the hypocrites themselves.
The word fi'a means a group. Its pattern is fi'la, derived from fay' (return), because members of a group turn to one another in their affairs. Its original form was fay', but the ya was omitted from its middle because of frequent use, and the ta was added in its place.
It is understood that division into two groups can only mean division over one of two possible positions. The context is a discussion of faith and unbelief; that is, "What is the matter with you that you are divided between those who declare them unbelievers and those who excuse them, and that you apply the rulings of faith or unbelief to them?" It is said that this verse was revealed concerning those who withdrew on the day of Uhud: Abd Allah ibn Ubayy and his followers. The Muslims differed over whether to describe them as believers or unbelievers because of what they had done. In Sahih al-Bukhari, Zayd ibn Thabit reported: "Some people returned from Uhud, among the Companions of the Prophet, and the people split into two groups concerning them: one group said, 'Kill them,' and another said, 'No.' Then the verse 'What is the matter with you that you have become two groups concerning the hypocrites' was revealed." He also said, "It is Taybah; it expels filth just as


[Page 219]

"The fire removes the dross from silver"—that is, the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) did not kill them, acting according to the outward appearance of their condition, since they professed Islam. The verse thus explains that there should have been no hesitation concerning their case. Mujahid related that it was revealed about a group of people from Mecca who professed faith and migrated to Medina, then asked permission to return to Mecca to bring back merchandise in which they could trade. They claimed that they had remained believers, and the Muslims differed over their case: were they polytheists or Muslims? This is clarified by what is related from Ibn Abbas: it was revealed about a group of people from Mecca who concealed polytheism while professing Islam to the Muslims, so that they would be safe from the Muslims waging war against them when they went out on trading journeys or the like. The Muslims had heard that they had left Mecca on a trading journey, and a group of Muslims said:
We should ride out to them and fight them. Another group said, "How can we kill them when they have professed Islam?" The Muslims differed over this, and the Messenger of Allah did not censure either group until the verse was revealed. Al-Dahhak related that it was revealed concerning a people who had outwardly embraced Islam in Mecca but had not emigrated, and who supported the polytheists against the Muslims. They were the ones of whom Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Indeed, those whom the angels take in death while they are wronging themselves will say, "What were you doing?"} [al-Nisa: 97]—to the end of the verse. I think all these groups were known at the time the verse was revealed, and thus served as examples of its general purport: it applies to them and to others among all those known to be hypocrites at that time, whether from the people of Medina or the people of Mecca...... It seems that the verse was revealed after the time for fighting them had passed, with the aim of avoiding any confrontation with them when they set out, thereby drawing them along until the day of the conquest of Mecca.


[Page 220]

In all these possibilities, the point of blame is the error in ijtihad, owing to the weakness of the evidence relied upon by those who were mistaken. For the indications of the disbelief of those under discussion outweighed the evidence for their Islam, which consisted merely of uttering the word of Islam, while refraining from showing allegiance to the Muslims. This verse is evidence that when a mujtahid relies on weak evidence of a kind that a scholar would not ordinarily use as proof, he is not far from the possibility of being...
the blame—in this world—for making an error in something in which people of knowledge do not err.
The clause "and Allah has cast them back because of what they have earned" is circumstantial, meaning: if you have differed concerning them, Allah has already returned them to their evil state, because arkas means "to return to riks," and riks is close in meaning to rijs.
In the hadith in Sahih concerning dung, it says: "This is filth."
It is also said that the meaning of "he caused him to relapse" is "he turned him back," that is, he returned him in a vile manner; this is close in meaning to the first interpretation. Allah made their return to disbelief a recompense for their corrupt belief and their lack of sincerity toward His Messenger (peace and blessings be upon him). For deeds beget others of their own kind: a righteous deed brings an increase in righteous deeds, while an evil deed leads to the utmost sins. For this reason, the Qur'an repeatedly reports that deeds are a cause of attaining the utmost ends of their own kind.
And His saying, "Do you wish to guide those whom Allah has led astray?" is an explanatory resumption arising from the reproach and astonishment in His saying, "What is the matter with you that you have become two parties regarding the hypocrites?" For the listeners await an explanation of the basis for the reproach and wonder what stance they should take toward these hypocrites. The interrogative, which is reproachful in tone, is tinged with...


[Page 221]

by reproaching them for a suppressed clause that is the subject of an explanatory resumption. Its implied wording is: "Allah has indeed led them astray; would you then guide those whom Allah has led astray?" If this interpretation is sound, this verse is as explicit as a text in excusing one who offers an excuse on the basis of a mistaken interpretation. These people merely supposed that someone who was in fact an unbeliever was a Muslim whom it was impermissible to fight, interpreting his outward circumstances as indicating this. Yet neither Allah nor His Messenger declared them unbelievers. It is authentically reported that he (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about the believers' seeing their Lord, Blessed and Exalted is He. He said, "Do you have any difficulty seeing the sun on a clear day at midday, with no cloud before it?" They said, "No." He did not declare them unbelievers for not knowing about the vision, and this is a matter of belief. It is authentically reported that he (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked, "Where was our Lord before the heavens and the earth were created?" He did not object to the questioner, but said, "He was in a cloud, with air above it and air below it." Ahmad related this. It is authentically reported that he (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about the beginning of the creation of this world. He replied, "Allah existed, and there was nothing besides Him; His Throne was upon the water, and He wrote everything in the Record." Al-Bukhari related this. This is a matter of belief.
It is authentically reported that he (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked, "Where will people be on the Day when the earth is changed?" He replied, "On the Bridge." In another wording: "They will be in the darkness before the Bridge." He was then asked, "Who will be the first people to cross?" He replied, "The poor among the Emigrants." Muslim related it. There is no contradiction between the two answers, for the darkness is at the beginning of the Bridge: there the changing begins, and it is completed while they are on the Bridge. This is a matter of belief, and he did not declare them unbelievers.
.


[Page 222]

"The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about the people of a household among the polytheists who were attacked at night, with some of their children and women being struck. He said, 'They are of them.' This is an authentic hadith.

When the words of Allah, Exalted is He, were revealed: {Indeed, you will die, and indeed, they will die} [al-Zumar: 30] {Then, on the Day of Resurrection, you will dispute before your Lord} [al-Zumar: 31], the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked, 'Messenger of Allah, will what happened between us in this world be brought up again, even with regard to particular sins?' He replied, 'Yes, it will surely be brought up again until you give everyone who is entitled to a right their due.' Al-Zubayr said, 'By Allah, this is indeed a grave matter.' The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) was also asked about medicines and incantations, whether they avert anything decreed by destiny. He replied, 'They are part of destiny.'"

"The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about those of the children of the polytheists who die. He replied, 'Allah knows best what they would have done.'"

Some of the Companions interpreted the word 'wrongdoing' in the words of Allah, Exalted is He, {and have not mixed their faith with wrongdoing} to mean every kind of wrongdoing. This was a matter of belief, and it troubled them greatly, so they asked, 'Which of us has not wronged himself?' The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) replied, 'That is not what it means; it means only polytheism. Have you not heard what Luqman said to his son: {My son, do not associate anything with Allah. Indeed, associating others with Allah is a tremendous wrongdoing} [Luqman: 13]?' He did not declare them unbelievers for this interpretation or belief of theirs.

"The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) was also asked about soothsayers. He said, 'They are nothing.' The questioner said, 'Sometimes they tell us something, and it comes to pass.' He replied, 'That is ...'


[Page 223]

"A word of truth is snatched by a jinni and cast into the ear of his human ally, and they mix with it a hundred lies." Agreed upon. Here, some of the Companions were unaware that Allah alone possesses knowledge of the unseen, and that He may disclose some of it to certain people, such as soothsayers. The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) informed them that this was not correct, but did not declare them disbelievers. This is a matter of creed. Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds, and Allah knows best.

(How the proof is established)
The question now is: how is the proof established against a person, such that establishing it confirms the legal ruling of disbelief against him, and consequently the rulings applicable to disbelievers are applied to him? We said earlier that there are categories of people who are not excused, despite affiliating themselves with Islam and bearing witness to the two testimonies. They include everyone who falls short in seeking religious knowledge, thereby becoming ignorant, and then begins to fabricate lies about Allah without knowledge, associating others with Him in supplication, vows, or otherwise, without knowledge—whether he invented this himself or followed someone he takes as an authority without proof, since following another is no excuse. Rather, if he did not know, he was obliged to believe in Allah in a general way and entrust to Allah, Glorified and Exalted, what he did not know. The second category likewise includes everyone who comes to hold a belief, but whose belief results from a false interpretation unsupported by either the Sharia or the language. I have already explained false interpretation and that it is not accepted. The third category is one that has fallen into disbelief as a result of an interpretation that has some basis in language or the Sharia. In this case, the proof is established against him.


[Page 224]

The proof. But how is the proof established? By induction from the verses and the hadiths of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), we can derive some indications of how to establish the proof and how to know whether this man who is interpreting the texts is following his desires, or whether he seeks the truth and prefers Allah and His Messenger over all others, but has fallen into a kind of misinterpretation that led him to err on the issue at hand. The researcher will, Allah willing, present some of this evidence and comment on it.
{Say, "Then bring a Book from Allah that is a better guide than these two, and I will follow it, if you are truthful." (49) But if they do not respond to you, then know that they are only following their own desires. And who is more astray than one who follows his own desire without guidance from Allah? Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people. (50)}
Here Allah Most High specified for the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) how to distinguish one who follows his desires from one who gives precedence to the truth: by calling that person to present the opposing evidence. If you bring this person sound evidence from the Book or the Sunnah, then one of two things is incumbent upon him: either to follow it or to produce counterevidence. If he does neither, we then know that he follows his desires, and we can say with a clear conscience that proof has been established against this person.


[Page 225]

He is subject to the proof because of his turning away. There is no doubt that Allah has not charged us with what we cannot do, namely, knowing what lies within people; rather, He has given us signs that make these inner realities clear. Nor is there any doubt that He has not left the religion as a plaything for people, so that anyone may come along and say, "I am interpreting." No: whoever has had the proof established against him is judged accordingly and punished for the act. And Allah knows best.

2. {Indeed, those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah without any authority that has come to them have nothing in their breasts but pride, which they will never attain. So seek refuge in Allah; indeed, He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.} 56

This verse also clarifies who has the proof established against him. Whoever argues with you without clear evidence, whether following blind imitation or following evidence, this verse indicates that there is pride in his heart. If we return to the definition of pride, we find that it means rejecting and resisting the truth, and looking down on people. This need makes clear that one who speaks without evidence is, in reality, rejecting and resisting the truth, rather than seeking it or preferring it above all else. Thus the proof has been established against him, and the ruling applies to him.


[Page 226]

3—"Whoever invokes another god alongside Allah, for which he has no proof, his reckoning is only with his Lord. Indeed, the disbelievers will not succeed." 117

This verse clearly shows that anyone who associates another with Allah by invoking someone other than Him must produce proof for his action. Since he has produced no proof, the argument has thereby been established against him, and Allah will call him to account for it. He will be among the disbelievers who do not succeed. This verse demonstrates the gravity of associating partners with Allah.

4—"They said, 'If we follow the guidance with you, we will be snatched away from our land.' Have We not established for them a secure sanctuary to which the fruits of all things are brought as provision from Us? But most of them do not know." 57

"Then you see those in whose hearts is a disease hastening to them, saying, 'We fear that a turn of fortune may befall us.' Perhaps Allah will bring victory or a command from Him, and then they will regret what they had concealed within themselves. And those who believe will say, 'Are these the ones who swore …'" 52


[Page 227]

By Allah, with their strongest oaths, they swear that they are with you. Their deeds have come to nothing, and they have become losers.

These two verses show that their companions fell into disbelief and were not excused by claiming worldly interests as a reason for committing disbelief. Thus, anyone who justifies his disbelief by citing a worldly interest that he supposes to be valid, without evidence that the Sharia recognizes it as a barrier to declaring someone a disbeliever (such as coercion, for example), will not have that justification accepted, nor will it prevent the ruling from being applied to him.

4. "Or do they say, 'He has fabricated it'? Say, 'Then bring a surah like it, and call upon whoever you can besides Allah, if you are truthful.' Rather, they have denied what they have not comprehended in knowledge, and whose interpretation has not yet come to them. Thus did those before them deny. So see what was the end of the wrongdoers."

The discussion of this verse takes another angle: sometimes you present someone with sound proof from the Book or the Sunnah, and he finds no evidence to counter what you have said. As a result of following his own desires, he resorts to rejecting this proof and calling you a liar. Allah, Exalted is He, makes clear that whoever does this is a wrongdoer, and that we must not reject what we have not comprehended.


[Page 228]

and deny it. Whoever does this is driven by personal desire and a prior conviction about a particular matter that he wishes to establish. Otherwise, why would he reject the truth without knowledge? Such a person is an unjust wrongdoer with no excuse: he speaks about Allah without knowledge and denies His Sharia without knowledge. It is noteworthy that all the verses in which Allah condemns the polytheists concern those who speak about Him without knowledge or deny His Sharia without knowledge. As for the third category, the one who seeks Allah's pleasure, like Zayd ibn Amr ibn Nufayl, he refrains from affirming anything about Allah that he does not know, and from denying anything of Him that he does not know. Rather, he believes in Him, Exalted is He, with general faith. Such a person is excused for what he does not know, so long as he remains silent and refrains from taking a position. A hadith, deemed sound by some scholars, states that he will be resurrected as a nation unto himself. This matter is under study, Allah willing.
5- "They worship, besides Allah, that for which He has sent down no authority and of which they have no knowledge. The wrongdoers will have no helper."
This verse has the same meaning as the preceding verses, for Allah calls one who has neither proof nor knowledge of what he says a wrongdoer. This shows that anyone who speaks without knowledge or proof is sinful and unjust, and has no excuse.


[Page 229]

6—Why did they not produce four witnesses to it? Since they did not produce the witnesses, they are, in the sight of Allah, the liars. (13) Were it not for Allah's grace and mercy upon you in this world and the Hereafter, a tremendous punishment would have touched you for what you plunged into. (14)
7—When you passed it from tongue to tongue, and said with your mouths what you had no knowledge of, thinking it a trivial matter, while in the sight of Allah it was tremendous. (15)
This verse indicates that whoever speaks without knowledge has committed a grave matter and is not excused.
8—Or have We sent down to them an authority, so that it speaks of what they used to associate with Him?
9—And they made the angels, who are servants of the Most Merciful, females. Did they witness their creation? Their testimony will be recorded, and they will be questioned. (19)


[Page 230]

This verse also makes clear that the polytheists will be called to account for making this claim without knowledge, since they testified to something they did not know.
10. We did not create the heavens and the earth and all that is between them except in truth and for an appointed term. Yet those who disbelieve turn away from what they have been warned of. Say, "Consider those whom you invoke besides Allah: show me what they have created of the earth, or do they have any share in the heavens? Bring me a scripture from before this, or some remnant of knowledge, if you are truthful."
The verse indicates that whoever offers no proof or knowledge for what he says is not truthful. And since there are only the truthful and the liars in this world, he is a liar in what he claims and is not excused.
11. Say, "Consider: if this is from Allah and you disbelieve in it, while a witness from among the Children of Israel testifies to something like it and believes, whereas you are arrogant—indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people."


[Page 231]

Here, establishing the proof came from another angle: the faith of those whose knowledge and testimony they trusted in the soundness of the proof. Sometimes you present someone with proof from an authentic report or from the Sunnah of the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), and he says to you, for example, "I follow such-and-such scholar, whom I trust." So you present him with that scholar's statement affirming the soundness of the evidence you cited and acting in accordance with its implications. If he still does not submit, then the proof has been established against him. This indicates his arrogance and wrongdoing, and that he is not excused on the grounds of his interpretation. For Allah cited as proof against them the faith of their leading scholar, Abdullah ibn Salam. This was therefore a sound argument and a valid counterargument that exposed the matter in establishing the proof.
12. And when you said, "O Moses, we will never believe you until we see Allah plainly," then the thunderbolt seized you while you looked on. Then We raised you to life after your death, so that you might give thanks.
This is evidence that excessive demands for proofs, after they have been made clear, are not valid. For when you cite the Book of Allah and


[Page 232]

The Sunnah of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) is sufficient for this, and no one is excused for rejecting it. Otherwise, Allah would have excused these people and would not have struck them down.

13. And if they see a sign, they turn away and say, "An ongoing magic."
This verse is likewise relevant.

14. Or do they say, "He has fabricated it"? Say, "Then bring ten fabricated surahs like it, and call upon whomever you can besides Allah, if you are truthful." But if they do not respond to you, then know that it has been sent down only with Allah's knowledge, and that there is no god but He. Will you then submit?
Here Allah, Exalted is He, made the man's truthfulness contingent upon producing countervailing evidence. This shows that whoever is unable to do so and fails to produce countervailing evidence is lying in his claim and following his own desire.


[Page 233]

58 Say, "Have you considered what Allah has sent down for you as provision, of which you have made some unlawful and some lawful?" Say, "Has Allah given you permission, or are you fabricating lies against Allah?" 59 What, then, do those who fabricate lies against Allah think will happen to them on the Day of Resurrection? Indeed, Allah is gracious to people, but most of them are not grateful. 60 Thus Allah made the one who speaks about religion without knowledge one of two men: a man whom Allah has permitted to speak in this way, or a man who fabricates lies against Allah. Allah threatened this speaker without knowledge with what he will face on the Day of Resurrection. This shows that this is neither an excuse nor a barrier to establishing the proof against him.

And those whom they invoke besides Allah do not follow partners; they follow nothing but conjecture, and they do nothing but guess. 66

They say, "Allah has taken a son." Exalted is He! He is Self-Sufficient; to Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on the earth. You have no authority for this. Do you say about Allah what you do not know? 68 Say, "Indeed, those who fabricate lies..."


[Page 234]

Upon Allah, a lie: they will not prosper. 69. A provision in this world; then to Us will be their return, and then We will make them taste the severe punishment for their disbelief. 70. The apparent meaning of this verse is that Allah, Exalted is He, called the one who fabricates lies against Allah a disbeliever and threatened him with punishment. It is also authentically reported that suspicion is the most false of speech, as the Messenger (peace and blessings be upon him) said. Thus, the Messenger called speech based on doubt a lie. Therefore, anyone who speaks without knowledge, saying something he doubts, is fabricating lies against Allah and is not excused, because he has followed that of which he has no knowledge.

And He, Exalted is He, said: "These are our people, who have taken gods besides Him. Why do they not bring clear authority for them? So who is more unjust than one who fabricates a lie against Allah?" He thus called one who brings no clear proof a fabricator of lies against Allah, and one in such a state is not excused.(15)

Moses said to them, "Woe to you! Do not fabricate lies against Allah, lest He destroy you with a punishment. Whoever fabricates lies has indeed failed." 61.


[Page 235]

"So what makes you deny the Judgment?"—after mentioning the signs of the creation of man in the best form.

Ibn Abbas reported that the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Do not boast of your forefathers who died in the pre-Islamic era. By Him in whose hand is my soul, the dung beetle rolling dung with its nostrils is better than your forefathers who died in the pre-Islamic era." (2)

This is an authentic hadith. As for the meaning of "the dung beetle rolls," they said: that is, the beetle rolls excrement. This hadith clearly indicates that those who speak about Allah without knowledge are not excused.

Abraham's argument with his people

۞ And when Abraham said to his father, Azar, "Do you take idols as gods? I see that you and your people are clearly astray." 74 And thus We showed Abraham the dominion of the heavens and the earth, so that he might be among those who are certain. 75 When night covered him, he saw a star and said, "This is my Lord." But when it set, he said, "I do not love those that set." 76 Then when he saw the moon rising, he said, "This is my Lord." But when it set, he said, "If my Lord does not guide me, I will surely be among the people who are astray." 77 Then when he saw the sun rising, he said, "This is my Lord."


[Page 236]

"My Lord, this is greater." Then, when it set, he said, "O my people, I am free of what you associate with Allah. I have turned my face, as one inclining to the truth, toward Him who created the heavens and the earth, and I am not one of the polytheists." His people argued with him. He said, "Do you argue with me about Allah, when He has guided me? I do not fear what you associate with Him, unless my Lord wills something. My Lord encompasses all things in knowledge. Will you not then take heed? How could I fear what you associate with Him, when you do not fear associating with Allah that for which He has sent down no authority to you? Which of the two parties is more entitled to security, if you know? Those who believe and do not taint their faith with wrongdoing—for them is security, and they are rightly guided. That was Our argument, which We gave to Abraham against his people. We raise in degrees whom We will. Indeed, your Lord is Wise, Knowing." (78–83)
Abraham did not mention a miracle here. Rather, he argued against them that these things they worship were created and set, so how could they be worshipped? This showed that they had associated partners with Allah without any "authority" or proof, and he made this a sufficient reason for not being secure from Allah's punishment. This shows that it is a sufficient proof, and that there is no excuse for anyone who rejects it.


[Page 237]

And the Exalted said: "Among the people is one who disputes about Allah without knowledge, guidance, or an illuminating Book, turning his neck aside to lead others away from the way of Allah. For him there is disgrace in this world, and on the Day of Resurrection We shall make him taste the punishment of the Burning Fire. That is for what your hands have sent ahead, and Allah is never unjust to His servants."

And indeed, We sent Noah to his people, and he said, "O my people, worship Allah. You have no god other than Him. I fear for you the punishment of a tremendous Day."

"Say, 'To whom belongs the earth and whoever is in it, if you know?' They will say, 'To Allah.' Say, 'Will you not then take heed?' Say, 'Who is the Lord of the seven heavens and the Lord of the Mighty Throne?' They will say, 'To Allah.' Say, 'Will you not then be mindful of Him?' Say, 'In whose hand is the dominion of all things, who protects, while none can protect against Him, if you know?' They will say, 'To Allah.' Say, 'How then are you deluded?'"

In these verses, Allah rebukes them for knowing that Allah is the owner of everything, yet not singling Him out in worship. Instead, they worshipped others without knowledge, following conjecture. This shows that this alone suffices as an argument against an opponent.

"There are three kinds of judges: two judges are in the Fire, and one judge is in Paradise. As for the one in Paradise, he is a man who knew the truth and judged accordingly, so he is in Paradise. A man knew the truth but did not judge by it and acted unjustly in his judgment; he is in the Fire. And a man who did not know the truth and judged among people out of ignorance; he is destined for the Fire."


[Page 238]

This hadith is clear evidence for what we are establishing: this was a judge who issued a ruling, but issued it out of ignorance. His ignorance here was no excuse; rather, Allah, Exalted is He, will punish him, because his duty was to remain silent about what he did not know, not to invent a new ruling. The hadith thus shows that ignorance is not an excuse in every circumstance.

Al-Nawawi said in his commentary on Sahih Muslim, in his discussion of the hadith, "When a judge rules and exercises independent reasoning, then gets it wrong, he has one reward": The scholars said: The Muslims are unanimously agreed that this hadith concerns a judge who is learned and qualified to judge. If he gets it right, he has two rewards: one for his independent reasoning and one for getting it right. If he gets it wrong, he has one reward for his independent reasoning. They said: As for someone who is not qualified to judge, it is not permissible for him to judge. If he does so, he earns no reward; rather, he is sinful, and his ruling is not enforceable, whether or not it accords with the truth. This is because his getting it right is merely coincidental, not based on a legal foundation. He is therefore disobedient in all his rulings, whether or not they are correct; all of them are rejected, and he is excused in none of them. End quote.

Establishing proof through the Book
{She said, "O chiefs, a noble letter has been delivered to me. It is from Solomon, and it reads: 'In the name of Allah, the Most Compassionate, the Most Merciful. Do not exalt yourselves above me, but come to me in submission.'" She said, "O chiefs, advise me concerning my affair. I do not decide any matter until you are present." They said, "We are men of strength and great might, but the decision is yours, so consider what you will command." She said, "When kings enter a town, they ruin it and make its most honored people ...}


[Page 239]

"Humiliated. And thus they do." "I shall send them a gift, and see what the messengers bring back." When he came to Solomon, he said, "Are you offering me wealth? What Allah has given me is better than what He has given you. Rather, it is you who rejoice in your gift. Return to them: we shall surely come to them with forces they cannot withstand, and drive them out from it, humbled and abased." Here Solomon made the mere act of writing to these people—who worshipped the sun and the moon and falsely attributed their worship to Allah—calling them to faith and monotheism sufficient grounds for punishment.
"He is nothing but a man who has fabricated a lie against Allah, and we will not believe him."
Ibn Hazm said regarding the manner of disputation and establishing proof:
"Al-Ihkam fi Usul al-Ahkam - Ibn Hazm" (1/21):
"Allah, Exalted is He, has commanded us explicitly in the Quran to follow the way of Abraham (peace be upon him), and Allah has informed us that disputation and debate were part of Abraham's way: at one time with the king, and at another with his people, using reasoning, as Allah has informed us. Thus, it is incumbent upon us to follow the practice of debate, so that we may turn the people of falsehood toward the truth, and to seek what is correct through reasoning concerning matters over which people disagree. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: {Indeed, the people most worthy of Abraham are those who followed him, and this Prophet and those who believe; and Allah is the Guardian of the believers.} We follow Abraham (peace be upon him) in disputation and debate, and so we are the people most worthy of him; all other people are commanded to do likewise. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Say, 'Allah has spoken the truth, so follow the way of Abraham, inclining toward truth; he was not one of the polytheists.'} Debate, as we have mentioned, is part of his way. Whoever forbids debate and proof should know that he is disobeying Allah, Mighty and Majestic, and opposing the way of Abraham and Muhammad (peace and blessings be upon them). Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said, praising the Companions of the Cave..."


[Page 240]

{We relate to you their story in truth. They were young men who believed in their Lord, and We increased them in guidance.} Thus Allah, Mighty and Majestic, praised them for rejecting the claim of their people, since their people had not established clear proof for what they said. And He, Exalted is He, affirmed their statement that whoever makes a claim without evidence is fabricating a lie against Allah, Mighty and Majestic.

And He, Exalted is He, said: {Who is more unjust than one who is reminded of the signs of his Lord and then turns away from them? Indeed, We will take retribution on the criminals.} No one is more unjust than one against whom proof has been established from the Book of Allah, Exalted is He, and from the words of His Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him), yet turns away from it, though it is the decisive proof and the clear argument. And Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Those who consume usury will not stand except as one stands who is being beaten by Satan into madness. That is because they say, "Trade is just like usury." But Allah has permitted trade and forbidden usury. Whoever receives an admonition from his Lord and desists may keep what is past, and his affair rests with Allah. But whoever returns to it—those are the inhabitants of the Fire; they will abide therein forever.} And He, Exalted is He, said: {Rather, those who do wrong follow their desires without knowledge. So who can guide one whom Allah has led astray? They will have no helpers.} Thus He, Exalted is He, has informed us, as you hear, that whoever follows a statement that accords with his view without knowing that it is sound is unjust, and that whoever does not return to the truth he hears is among the people of the Fire. And He, Exalted is He, said: {If they do not respond to you, then know that they follow only their desires. And who is more astray than one who follows his desire without guidance from Allah? Indeed, Allah does not guide the wrongdoing people.} Allah, Exalted is He, condemned a person for denying what he does not know, saying: {Rather, they denied what they had not encompassed in knowledge, and whose interpretation had not yet come to them. Thus those before them denied the truth. So see what was the end of the wrongdoers.} All that we have mentioned thus establishes the duty to refrain from what we do not know and to return to what proof requires once it has been established. And He, Exalted is He, said: {Who is more unjust than one who fabricates a lie against Allah or denies the truth when it comes to him? Is there not a dwelling in Hell for the disbelievers?} Abu Muhammad said: This verse suffices to establish that no one should affirm anything unless proof has been established for it, and that no one should do anything when proof has been established against it. Who, then, is more unjust than one who knows what we have mentioned, yet follows a whisper arising within himself, or a report for which no proof has been established that it must be believed, or


[Page 241]

He imitated a human being like himself, who may, in the sight of Allah, Exalted is He, be other than he supposes. In any case, he is not infallible; he may err or be right. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {And they say, "None shall enter Paradise unless he is a Jew or a Christian." Those are their wishful claims. Say, "Bring your proof, if you are truthful"}
"truthful." Thus, Exalted is He, made it incumbent upon anyone who is truthful in his claim to produce proof; and if he does not produce it,
On the basis of proof, he is a liar, one who invalidates the truth, or an ignorant person. Allah, Exalted is He, said: {Here you are, disputing about that of which you have knowledge; why then do you dispute about that of which you have no knowledge? Allah knows, and you do not know.} Thus, He, Exalted is He, permitted disputation only on the basis of knowledge and forbade it without knowledge.
And the Exalted says: {They will say, "Three, the fourth of them being their dog," and they will say, "Five, the sixth of them being their dog," guessing at the unseen; and they will say, "Seven, and the eighth of them being their dog." Say, "My Lord knows best their number. None knows them except a few. So do not argue about them except with an outward argument, and do not ask any of them about them."} Abu Muhammad said: When we found that Allah, Exalted is He, had commanded argumentation and debate in the verses we have mentioned, and had made the acceptance of anything obligatory only on the basis of proof, we were obliged to seek out blameworthy argumentation, as we explained earlier. We found that He said: {We send the messengers only as bearers of glad tidings and warners. But those who disbelieve argue with falsehood in order to refute the truth by it, and they take My signs and the warnings given to them in mockery.} Thus, as you can see, He condemned arguing without proof and arguing about falsehood. By this, Allah refuted the saying of the deluded: "Every deluded person is taught an argument." He made clear that the deluded person is one who is not taught an argument, and that the one who is right is, in truth, the one taught an argument; they are the people of truth. And the Exalted says: {Those who argue about the signs of Allah without any authority having come to them—it is greatly hateful in the sight of Allah and in the sight of those who believe. Thus Allah seals every arrogant tyrant's heart.} These verses together make clear what blameworthy argumentation is.


[Page 242]

The praiseworthy disputation is obligatory. It is the disputation in which the person engaging in it seeks to make the truth manifest. The blameworthy kind has two forms, as stated in the verses we have cited: one is disputing without knowledge, and the other is disputing in support of falsehood, through contentiousness and deception, after the truth has become clear to him. This shows that the truth is one, and that nothing is established except by a proof grounded in reason. These blameworthy people are those of whom Allah, Exalted is He, says: {Have you not seen those who dispute concerning the signs of Allah? How are they turned away?} And His saying: {Among the people is one who disputes concerning Allah without knowledge and follows every rebellious devil.} And His saying: {Among the people is one who disputes concerning Allah without knowledge, guidance, or an illuminating Book, turning his neck aside to lead others astray from the way of Allah. For him there is disgrace in this world, and on the Day of Resurrection We shall make him taste the punishment of the burning Fire.} And by His saying:
Exalted is He: {None dispute the signs of Allah except those who disbelieve, so do not let their movement through the lands deceive you. The people of Noah denied the truth before them, as did the confederates after them.
And every nation plotted against their messenger, to seize him, and argued falsely in order to refute the truth by means of it. Then I seized them; so how was My punishment?} Thus, as you see, Exalted is He makes clear that the forbidden disputation is that by which one argues to support falsehood and invalidate the truth without knowledge. Abu Muhammad said: It may be said to one who refuses to engage in disputation and undertake the pursuit of proof: Pharaoh said, {O my people, today you hold dominion, prevailing in the land. But who will protect us from Allah's might if it comes upon us?} Pharaoh said, {I show you only what I see, and I guide you only to the path of right guidance.} {And the one who believed said, "O my people, follow me; I will guide you to the path of right guidance."} By what means can one distinguish which of them is in the right and which is in the wrong? Is it permissible to know this by anything other than evidence apart from their words? This is the speech of the Almighty, the Irresistible, the Creator, the Originator. We have cited it as proof for following evidence and rejecting the claim of one who has no proof in hand. This is what no Muslim may oppose—not the saying of one who says, "Go to someone who doubts as you do, and debate him." It may be said to him: "Do you think ..."


[Page 243]

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) was in doubt, since his Lord, Exalted is He, taught him how to debate the People of the Book and the people of disbelief, and commanded him to seek proof and establish the argument against everyone who opposed him. Nor is there any validity to the statement of one who says, "Whenever a man comes who is more skilled in argument than another, should we abandon what we follow?" or words to that effect. Abu Muhammad said: This is a statement in which every atheist on earth is on equal footing with the one who makes it. If this speaker is permitted not to abandon what he found his forebears following, without proof, despite clear proof being presented to him, then the Jew and the Christian must likewise be permitted not to abandon what they found their forebears following in blind imitation, without proof, and not to accept the proof of Islam presented to them and its decisive argument. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, said: {They said, "O Lot, we are messengers of your Lord. They will never reach you. So set out with your family during a portion of the night, and let none of you look back, except your wife; what befalls them will befall her. Their appointed time is the morning. Is the morning not near?" * When Our command came, We turned it upside down and rained upon it stones of baked clay, one after another, * marked by your Lord; and it is not far from the wrongdoers. * And to Midian We sent their brother Shuayb. He said, "My people, worship Allah; you have no god other than Him..."}
"And do not give short measure or weight. Indeed, I see you in prosperity, and I fear for you the punishment of an all-encompassing Day. And, O my people, give full measure and weight with justice, and do not deprive people of their due, nor spread corruption in the land. What Allah leaves for you is better for you, if you are believers; and I am not a keeper over you. They said, 'O Shuayb, do your prayers command you that we abandon what our forefathers worship, or that we do as we please with our wealth? Indeed, you are the forbearing, the right-minded one.' He said, 'O my people, consider: if I have clear proof from my Lord and He has provided me with good provision from Him, I do not wish to do the very thing I forbid you to do. I desire only to set things right as far as I can. My success is only through Allah; in Him I put my trust, and to Him I turn. And, O my people, do not let your hostility toward me bring upon you the like of what befell the people of Noah, or the people of Hud, or the people of Salih; and the people of Lot are not far from you. Seek forgiveness from your Lord, then turn to Him in repentance. Indeed, my Lord is Merciful, Loving.' They said, 'O Shuayb, we do not understand much of what you say, and we see you as weak among us. Were it not for your clan, we would have stoned you; you have no standing with us."} Abu Muhammad said: Since Allah has thus urged you,


[Page 244]

Exalted is He, who urged disputation on the basis of truth and commanded the seeking of proof. It is established, by the text we have cited, that seeking evidence is the way of Allah, Mighty and Majestic. Whoever forbids this and turns people away from it is turning them away from the way of Allah, Exalted is He, and is an oppressor.

His saying, Exalted is He: {And when We appointed for Moses forty nights, then you took the calf after him, while you were wrongdoers. Then We pardoned you after that, so that you might be grateful. And when We gave Moses the Book and the Criterion, so that you might be guided. And when Moses said to his people, "My people, you have wronged yourselves by taking the calf. So repent to your Creator and slay yourselves. That is better for you with your Creator." Then He accepted your repentance. Indeed, He is the Accepting of repentance, the Merciful.}

They were not excused on the grounds that they had followed the Samaritan when he said to them, "This is your god and the god of Moses, but he forgot."

Sahih al-Bukhari (6/14): It is related that


[Page 245]

Aisha said: "We administered the ladud to him during his illness, and he began gesturing to us, 'Do not administer the ladud to me.' We said, 'The sick person's aversion to medicine.' When he came to, he said, 'Did I not forbid you to administer the ladud to me?' We said, 'The sick person's aversion to medicine.' He said, 'Let no one in the house remain without being given the ladud while I watch, except al-Abbas, for he was not present with you.'"
This shows that someone who offers a far-fetched interpretation is not excused, because the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) punished them. The ladud is medicine placed in one side of the mouth, or administered by rubbing it onto the palate, inserting a finger into the patient's mouth and applying the medicine to it. The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) explicitly forbade them from doing this by gesturing, but they assumed that he did not mean to forbid it, and merely disliked the medicine. This was a far-fetched assumption. The hadith also shows that a prohibition indicates unlawfulness, and that a far-fetched interpretation is not accepted.
"Sahih al-Bukhari" (1/31):
Abdullah ibn Amr reported: The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) fell behind us during a journey we had undertaken. He caught up with us when we had delayed the prayer—the Asr prayer—and we were performing ablution. We began wiping our feet, so he called out at the top of his voice, "Woe to the heels from the Fire!" twice or three times.
In this hadith, the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) did not excuse them, because the matter of ablution was well known. And Allah knows best.
Contradiction
By this heading, I mean that a person's contradiction indicates that he follows his own desires and is not excused. For example, a man says that a mursal hadith is sound. Then you want to establish proof against him, so you bring him a mursal hadith with a sound chain of transmission. He says to you, "This is mursal; I do not accept it because it is weak." Such a person is contradictory and is not excused, and proof has thereby been established against him.


[Page 246]

Allah Most High said concerning the Jews:
"Those who said, 'Allah has bound us by a covenant not to believe in any messenger until he brings us an offering that fire consumes.' Say, 'Messengers before me came to you with clear proofs and with what you have asked for, so why did you kill them, if you are truthful?'"
Thus He made clear that they were lying in their claim that the reason they did not believe was that this sign and proof had not appeared. For this proof had already existed before, yet they still did not believe. Their contradiction therefore showed that they were lying and had no excuse. 183
Allah Most High said: "When the truth came to them from Us, they said, 'Why was he not given the like of what Moses was given?' Did they not disbelieve in what Moses had been given before? They said, 'Two works of magic supporting one another,' and they said, 'We disbelieve in them all.'"
This verse is like the one before it.

(The reason for declaring the one who excuses them to be an unbeliever)
The question now is: What is the reason for declaring the one who excuses them to be an unbeliever? They say that the one who excuses them is now lying about Allah and His Messenger, since by excusing this person who errs in interpretation in what they call matters of creed, known by innate disposition, he is as though saying that Allah and His Messenger have lied by judging this person to be an unbeliever, when in fact he is a believing monotheist. The answer is that he never intended to deny Allah and His Messenger. Rather, in their view, someone who adopts an interpretation that has some basis is excused for his error, and this is the ruling of Allah and His Messenger concerning them; and, in his view, they are truthful in this ruling. Thus there is no basis here for declaring the one who excuses them to be an unbeliever. At the very least, he is interpreting their Islam charitably, for the sake of argument.


[Page 247]

The early scholars differed over declaring many groups to be unbelievers. Yet the one who declared them unbelievers did not say to the one who did not, "You have denied Allah and His Messenger, since they judged this act to be unbelief, and you have denied them by saying that this act is not unbelief."

Here I will mention some individuals over whose status the Sunnis differed. It is stated in al-I'tisam by al-Shatibi, edited by al-Shaqir, al-Humayd, and al-Sini (1/223):
"For the early scholars and others differed over declaring many of their sects to be unbelievers, such as the Kharijites, the Qadariyya, and others."

It is stated in al-Sawa'iq al-Muhriqa 'ala Ahl al-Rafd wa al-Dalal wa al-Zandaqa (2/630):
"Know that the Sunnis differed over declaring Yazid ibn Mu'awiya and his successor after him to be unbelievers. One group said that he was an unbeliever, citing the well-known account related by Sibt ibn al-Jawzi and others: when the head of al-Husayn (may Allah be pleased with him) was brought, he gathered the people of Syria, began striking the head with a cane, and recited verses by Ibn al-Ziba'ra."

It is stated in Mirqat al-Mafatih Sharh Mishkat al-Masabih (4/1641):
"They differed over declaring someone who is ignorant of one of the attributes of Allah, Exalted is He, to be an unbeliever. Qadi Iyad said: Among those who declared him an unbeliever was Ibn Jarir al-Tabari, and Abu al-Hasan al-Ash'ari initially held this view. Others said that he is not declared an unbeliever on this account, unlike one who denies the attribute. Abu al-Hasan later returned to this view, and it became his settled position. He said: This is because the person holds this belief with certainty that he is right and regards it as a religious ruling; only someone who believes that his own doctrine is true is declared an unbeliever."


[Page 248]

It is stated in
((Al-Urf al-Shadhi, Commentary on Sunan al-Tirmidhi (1/81)

They differed over declaring the Rafidah unbelievers. The Hanafis have two opinions: it was said that they are unbelievers, and it was said that they are not. The preferred view is that they are unbelievers, for whoever declares the majority of the Companions unbelievers is an unbeliever, and the Rafidah restrict Islam to nine or seven Companions.))

It is stated in
((Sharh al-Tahawiyya - Yusuf al-Ghufays (19/18, numbered automatically by al-Maktaba al-Shamela)
On the disagreement over declaring one who abandons fasting and pilgrimage an unbeliever
As for fasting and pilgrimage, a group of the early generations held that abandoning fasting is unbelief, and a group of the early generations held that abandoning pilgrimage is unbelief. The vast majority of the early and later generations held that abandoning fasting alone or abandoning pilgrimage alone is not unbelief.))

((Ibn Hubayra said: "They differed concerning one who learns magic and practices it. Abu Hanifa, Malik, and Ahmad said that he thereby becomes an unbeliever. However, some of Abu Hanifa's followers made a distinction: if he learns it in order to guard against it or avoid it, he does not thereby become an unbeliever; but if he learns it believing it to be permissible, or believing that it will benefit him, then he becomes an unbeliever. He did not consider the ruling to be absolute. If he believes that the devils do whatever they wish, then he is an unbeliever. Al-Shafi'i said: 'If someone learns magic, we say to him: Describe your magic. If he describes something that entails unbelief, such as the belief of the people of Babylon that they draw near to the planets'


[Page 249]

The seven, and that they do what is sought of them, is a disbeliever. If he does not deem it to entail disbelief, then if he believes it to be permissible, he is a disbeliever.)
Disagreement over declaring one who abandons prayer to be a disbeliever is well known.
In all these issues, scholars differed over the individuals involved. According to some, they are disbelievers, abiding forever in the Fire; according to others, they are believers. The group that declared them disbelievers did not say that the other group were disbelievers for contradicting Allah and His Messenger by calling disbelief merely a sin. »»
Second: They said that the cause of declaring someone a disbeliever is ignorance of the true nature of Islam. The answer is that this same cause is present in issues over which the early generations differed. For example, one who declares a person who abandons prayer to be a disbeliever considers those who do not declare him a disbeliever to be in a state of compounded ignorance about the true nature of Islam, since, in his view, prayer is a pillar whose absence nullifies Islam altogether. The other side, by contrast, regards its being an obligation as having no bearing on the validity of Islam. Thus, this person is ignorant of the true nature of Islam, yet the early generations did not declare one another disbelievers on this basis. Abu Ubayda al-Jazairi himself said, relying on this very rationale—that the early generations did not declare one another disbelievers in certain issues—as evidence that we do not declare such a person a disbeliever: "One does not declare a person a disbeliever if he doubts or refrains from declaring one who abandons prayer a disbeliever, or refrains from declaring the Kharijites disbelievers; likewise, one who refrains or doubts regarding the disbelief of someone who has apostatized, but the grounds for it have not become clear to him.


[Page 250]

His disbelief, like that of Abu Hanifa and al-Hajjaj: the early generations differed over whether they were disbelievers, yet some did not declare others disbelievers." The question now is: if, by his own words, the early generations differed over declaring the Kharijites disbelievers, is this not clear evidence that they differed over the issue of declaring someone who interprets texts figuratively to be a disbeliever? Why, then, did you not declare those who did not declare the Kharijites disbelievers to be disbelievers, while you declared those who did not declare the Ash'aris disbelievers? The underlying cause in both cases is the same: declaring someone a disbeliever on the basis of figurative interpretation. If you excused those who excused the Kharijites, despite the existence of explicit hadiths declaring them disbelievers—hadiths of which there are none like them concerning the Ash'aris—and if we consider these hadiths and descriptions, we find that they contain rulings: that they are "the worst of creation," and "wherever you encounter them, kill them." Abu Umama, one of the Companions who narrated the hadith, said that they "disbelieved after their faith." These are three descriptions pertaining to this world; in the Hereafter, they are "the dogs of Hell." And he (peace and blessings be upon him) said, "They leave the religion and then never return to it." All these descriptions strongly indicate that these people are disbelievers who have left the religion. It is astonishing, then, to excuse those who did not declare these people disbelievers, despite the clarity of the texts concerning them, and then hasten to declare the Ash'aris disbelievers, those who do not declare the Ash'aris disbelievers, or those who do not declare an ignorant person who lacks knowledge of the attribute to be a disbeliever.... Moreover, among the condemnations of these Kharijites is what al-Subki says, as quoted in Fath al-Bari: "Those who declared the Kharijites and the extremist Rafidis disbelievers argued that they declared leading Companions to be disbelievers, which entails rejecting the Prophet's testimony that they would enter Paradise. He said: 'In my view, this is a sound argument.' He said: 'Those who did not declare them disbelievers argued that ruling them to be disbelievers would require that they had prior, certain knowledge of the aforementioned testimony.' This argument is open to question, for we know with certainty that those whom they declared disbelievers were upright until the time of their death.


[Page 251]

That is sufficient, in our view, to declare those who declare them unbelievers to be unbelievers. This is supported by the hadith, "If a man says to his brother, 'Unbeliever,' then it applies to one of them." In Muslim's wording: "Whoever accuses a Muslim of unbelief, or says, 'Enemy of Allah,' it will rebound upon him." He said: "It has been established that these people accuse a group of unbelief, people whose faith we know with certainty. They must therefore be judged unbelievers, in accordance with the implication of the Lawgiver's report."
Ibn Hazm likewise inclined to declare them unbelievers, and counted among their reprehensible acts, as stated in al-Muhalla bi al-Athar (11/334):
"It is thus established that interpretation differs. Any group that, in its rebellion, interprets the texts so as to erase some part of the Sunnah—such as those who adopt the view of the Kharijites in order to remove authority from Quraysh, or to return people to the view that stoning is invalid, or that people who commit sins are unbelievers, or that Muslims may be forced to lend money, or that children and women may be killed; or who openly profess that divine decree is invalid, or that the vision of Allah is impossible, or that Allah, Exalted is He, knows nothing until it comes to be; or who disavow some of the Companions, invalidate intercession, reject acting upon established Sunnahs from the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him), and call people to refer to someone other than the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him); or who call for withholding zakat, or withholding any right due to a Muslim, or any right due to Allah, Exalted is He—such people are not excused on the basis of corrupt interpretation, for this is utter ignorance."
These are some of the reprehensible acts of the Kharijites, of which we find not even a little among the Asharis, despite the sound hadiths that indicate that these people are unbelievers.
If the reason for declaring the one who excuses them an unbeliever is his ignorance of the true nature of Islam, as you claim, then you are necessarily obliged to declare those among the early generations who excused the Kharijites unbelievers, those who held that…


[Page 252]

with all these abominations, which contradict the reality of Islam and are opposed to it, despite the hadiths explicitly concerning these particular people and no others.
Third: Their claim that the reason for declaring the one who excuses someone acting on an interpretation to be an unbeliever is that he has opposed consensus. I do not know what they mean by this. If they mean that consensus arose among the early generations on declaring someone acting on an interpretation to be an unbeliever, then we have already cited the well-known disagreement on this matter, so there is no need to repeat it here. Moreover, they have recently contradicted themselves, since they acknowledge that there was disagreement among the early generations about excusing an interpretive Kharijite. So where, then, is the consensus?
If they say that the early generations agreed against the view of the Ash'aris and others who interpret the attributes out of ignorance, the answer is: yes. Likewise, we know of no disagreement among them about rejecting and repudiating what the Kharijites held concerning the matters mentioned, nor about rejecting what the Qadaris held. Yet despite these sects' opposition, disagreement arose among the early generations about declaring them unbelievers, and disagreement arose over whether or not to excuse them. This was because some members of these sects might not regard this consensus as sound, might consider it speculative, or might point to matters concerning the divine attributes for which almost nothing at all was transmitted from the Companions, either about the attributes themselves or their interpretation. They might also have a doubt based on a verse or hadith, as happened with the Ash'aris regarding the words of the Exalted: {There is nothing like Him}; this led them to interpret the attributes. I will give here an example of the disagreement over declaring the Kharijites unbelievers. In Fath al-Bari, Ibn Hajar says: "Shaykh Taqi al-Din al-Subki said in his fatwas: Those who declared the Kharijites and the extremist Rafidis unbelievers argued that they themselves declared leading Companions unbelievers, which entails accusing the Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) of lying when he testified that they would enter Paradise. He said: In my view, this is a sound argument. He said: Those who did not declare them unbelievers argued that judging them to be unbelievers requires that they had prior, certain knowledge of the testimony in question. This argument is open to objection, because we know that"


[Page 253]

The exoneration of those whom they declared unbelievers was established with definitive knowledge until their death. This suffices, in our view, to deem unbelievers those who declared them unbelievers. This is supported by the hadith, "If a man says to his brother, 'Unbeliever,' then one of the two has incurred it." In Muslim's wording: "Whoever accuses a Muslim of unbelief, or says, 'Enemy of Allah,' it will rebound upon him." He said: It has been established that these people accuse a group of unbelief, people whose faith we know with certainty; therefore, in accordance with the report of the Lawgiver, they must be judged unbelievers.
